It checks `IdentityBound`, `RevocationSound`, `FailClosed`, `NoBackdate`, `SeatPerPerson`, `SeatPerIdentifier`, `TerminalStable`, `StaleNeverUncounts`, `NoExecAfterKnownRevocation`, `FinalNeedsCompleteSeries`, `AbsenceOnlyInWindow`, `TallyClean`, `DeadStaysOut` and `EventuallyCovered` (coverage by a head only, not that the gate then counts the decision), with a pruning CA, a gate crash, two seats under revocation and validity intervals that start late, and each rule has a counterexample configuration that switches it off and fails with the property it guards (no freshness rule, no non-decreasing head times, no name constraints, no series coverage, no gate `Anchor` step, no pre-execution status check, no `L(C)` rule on the live count or the check, no tally recheck, no dead set). Model 1b gains a certificate-bound approver (a leaf whose validity can turn false at any step), the pre-execution status check on every path to the tool (`NoExecAfterKnownRevocation`, with crashes, redeploys and recovery, also for a path that skips the check), the identity re-ask that lets a replacement decision under another leaf execute after a revocation held the call (`ReplacementSupersedes`), and the decision relation (approver, verdict, decision time, leaf: two leaves are two decisions, two chains for one leaf are one; `NoSquat`, `OneRecordPerDecision`, each of the four fields in the name required by a configuration); its existing configurations are unchanged apart from three constants that default to nothing. `modelsync` now holds `audit` `VerifyChain`, `EvaluateStatus` and `AuditedStore.Reanchor` and `agent.TallyApprovals` to model 15's map. The models run in their own CI shards.
- How a gate with a certificate-bound approver names its tally and asks again, in the planned approver identity feature (no run writes an exec hold yet). Such a gate records its terminal tally under `approval-tally:<id>:ident` (`agent.ApprovalIdentTallyStep`) and treats a plain-name tally that passed as superseded: the call does not run on it, `Run` returns `*ApprovalPending` with the new `agent.PendingIdentReapproval`, and the new count supersedes it. A valid exec hold of generation `g` (`agent.ExecHold`) supersedes the passed generation-`g` tally, so the gate has no outcome until it counts generation `g+1` under `approval-tally:<id>:ident-reask:<g+1>` (`agent.PendingExecHoldReapproval`); a hold over a denial, with no tally under it, or of another call, generation or format is ignored. `agent.RecordedTally`, the gate, `agent.EvaluateGate`, the notifier (new event key `ApprovalIdentReaskKey`, decided names, journal events), `agent.LatestPauseNote` and `ValidReaskNote` (re-ask notes `agent.ApprovalIdentReaskStep`), the deadline check, `plan` `Conform`, and `audit.ApprovalEvidence` and `VerifyApprovals` (which now carry the superseded tallies, the holds and the notes) read the chain by one rule. A gate of bare-key approvers is unchanged. A certificate-bound gate whose count passed without excluding the requester refuses with `ErrConfig` if the policy then excludes it. A certificate-bound gate is also never counted with bare keys: whether a gate is bound is read from its journal (an identity tally, an exec hold, or a note with `identity_bound`) as well as from the policy, and a policy that lost the certificate-bound approvers or changed their kinds is refused with `ErrConfig`. The dead end of separation of duties turned on after a bound count wraps the new `agent.ErrGateNeedsNewRun`. A plan flow gate no longer takes a pass from a plain-name tally, and `SupersededTallies` lists the whole chain.
- The certificate-bound decision format of the planned approver identity feature (build step 1; the gate does not use it yet, so no behaviour changes for bare-key approvers). `agent.ApprovalDecisionBytesCert` (`bide.approval.v4`) is the v3 layout plus the SHA-256 of the signer's leaf certificate (`agent.CertLeafHash`); `agent.Decision.Cert` and the record member `approver_cert` carry the leaf-first chain (at most 8 certificates of 16 KiB), `SubmitDecision` journals it and adds the leaf hash to the decision's step name, and `WithDecisionCheck` refuses a decision that carries one until its verifier exists. A bare-key verifier never counts a v4 signature. `audit.DecisionReceipt` gains `cert`; `Verify` refuses a receipt that carries a chain (`audit.ErrCertificateBound`), and the new `VerifyCertificate(ci, logKey)` checks the chain and identifier at the time of the head the receipt's own anchoring proof carries (`AnchorTime`), then the signature under the leaf's key. A decision's identity includes its leaf in `WithDecisionCheck` and `HeldDecisionStep`.

### Changed

- `docs/KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md` no longer says that nothing in the approval gate uses certificate-bound approvers (it does, since the gate change), and the certificate-approvers guide links the "A person with two identifiers fills two seats" entry through its section, Approval and quorum.
- The run viewer guide's approver identity screenshots now come from records the real certificate-bound gate writes (a real audit.CertApprover on the agent runtime, in a test that runs only on request: cmd/bide/internal/identitydemo), not hand-written ones, and show only states the gate produces: a log-signer-clock final count, a provisional live count, a gate paused for revocation status, one waiting for a covering signed head, and a call paused before execution for current status. The viewer reads the identity records' RFC 3339 times with trimmed nanoseconds (they showed as "not a canonical time") and shows a counted gate whose newest phase note says exec-hold, with no hold record and no result, as paused for current status rather than final. The Needs attention list shows the bare-key approve command only for bare-key approvers and says so (and shows none when every pending approver is certificate-bound), and the demo test reads each run back and fails unless it is the state its caption says.
- `docs/ROADMAP.md` now lists only work that is not done: shipped items (approval deadlines, HSM and KMS key custody, signed releases and SBOMs, the notifier, the checked formal models, the mechanized gsm theorems) are removed, approver identity is marked in progress, and the visual builder lists `bide edit` as in progress.
- The certificate verification core in `audit` (`IdentityPolicy`, `CertIdentity`, `ApproverCert`, `CertError`, `SPKIHash`, the canonicalizer types) and the `agent` reasons `ReasonNoCert`, `ReasonCertChain`, `ReasonCertIdentity`, `ReasonCertUsage` and `ReasonCertWindow` are no longer labelled "building block, not yet used by approval gates" in the SDK API reference and the stability page: since the gate change for certificate-bound approvers, approval gates use them, so they carry their packages' label (core). `audit/rfc3161` keeps the building block label, since no gate or command calls it.

### Fixed

- `scripts/release.sh` no longer asks proxy.golang.org about a new module tag until the tag can be fetched directly (`GOPROXY=direct`, with `GOSUMDB=off`, so a miss caches nothing) and 300 seconds have passed since the push, then waits with growing intervals (5, 10, 15, 15 minutes) for at most 90 minutes (`PW_SETTLE`, `PW_STEP`, `PW_MAX`; `scripts/proxy-wait.sh`, also used by `scripts/module-sbom.sh`). In the v0.18.0 release every stage failed with "does not resolve": the proxy caches a miss for 30 to 60 minutes and polling every 30 seconds kept it fresh, so the `module-sbom` job gave up three minutes before the last module resolved. `release.yml` now gives that job a 270-minute timeout and `--wait 240`.
- The store/sqlite write-fairness tests no longer fail on a heavily loaded machine: they start every writer queued on the file's gate and read the commit order from the file, instead of timing goroutines. Test change only; the internal write gate gains a `Waiting` count that the tests use, and its behavior is unchanged.
- v0.18.0 did not publish the module set SBOM (`bide_0.18.0_modules.sbom.json`) that its release notes described: `scripts/module-sbom.sh` wrote the file relative to the temporary module it changes into, so the release workflow's signing step did not find it. The script is fixed in #343; the v0.18.0 release notes and `docs/trust/verify-releases.md` now say that v0.18.0 has no module set SBOM, and the next release carries it.

## [0.18.0] - 2026-10-16

### Added

- Generated SDK API reference: `docs/reference/api/` lists every exported constant, variable, function and type of every package in the published modules (the core module one page per package, every other module one page), each with its declaration and the first sentence of its doc comment, a pkg.go.dev link and a stability label (core, experimental, or building block, not yet used by approval gates, the last for `audit/rfc3161` and the certificate verification core in `audit`). It is generated from the Go sources with the standard library only by `internal/tools/apiref`; `TestAPIReference` fails when a page is stale (`go test ./internal/tools/apiref -run TestAPIReference -update` rewrites them), when an exported symbol has no doc comment, or when a package has no label, and `TestStabilityPage` fails when the new package table in `docs/trust/stability.md` differs from the labels the generator uses. The docs site lists the reference in its Reference sidebar.
- Approval deadlines: `agent.ApprovalPolicy.Timeout` (and `timeout` in a plan config's `approval` block) gives a gate a deadline, measured from the gate's recorded request and recorded once as `approval-deadline:<call>` (`agent.ApprovalDeadline`, `agent.RecordedDeadline`), so a redeploy does not move it. A gate past its deadline with no terminal count records its expiry as its outcome, insert-once under the name a passing tally or a single decision would take (`ApprovalTally.Expired`, `ExpiresAt`), so an expiry and a passing tally cannot both be recorded: the call never runs (the model reads `agent.ExpiredResult`; a plan flow returns `plan.ErrApprovalExpired`, which wraps `plan.ErrApprovalDenied`), a decision recorded after the deadline never counts (`agent.ReasonLate`), and `SubmitDecision`, `Approve`, the approvals endpoint (state `expired`, `expires_at`) and `bide approve` refuse a decision past it (`agent.ErrApprovalExpired`). `agent.EvaluateGate` reports `Expired` and `Deadline`. The notifier sends `approval_expired` and closes the pending approval with the reason `expired`. A decision written before a plan flow gate's request no longer blocks a single-approval gate: `Approve` records the real decision under the gate's second name, which the gate honours. Model 1b gains the expiry (`ExpiredNeverRuns`, `LateNeverCounts`, `ExpiryAfterDeadline`, `OutcomeOnce`, configurations `ap-deadline-m-cross` and `ap-deadline-one-cross`, regression `ap-late-counts`). Journal format: one new record name, and the tally and policy encodings gain optional fields, with no format version change before 1.0.
- Two run viewer guide screenshots show approval deadlines: the Needs attention list with an overdue gate, one due soon and one due tomorrow, and a run whose gate expired. `assets/demo/shots-deadline.sh` regenerates them from a small journal that `assets/demo/setup/deadline.go` writes with the real agent runtime and a controllable clock.
- Two runnable examples and the guide detail the v0.17.0 docs audit found missing. `examples/intake` mounts the approval intake (`WithGatePolicy`, `WithAuth` binding a bearer token to its approver, `WithPage`, `OnRecorded`) over an audited journal, takes a 2-of-3 refund gate with separation of duties through it, and checks the returned receipt against the signed heads; `examples/notify-routing` runs the notifier with routing by kind and approver, an escalation to a lead when on-call's endpoint is down, an all-clear, and a trail signed as it is written, and verifies it with `audit.VerifyNotifications`, which passes, fails on a trail with a record removed and reports the late acknowledgement. Both are in the root module, run offline and are tested. The approval guide now names `WithRateLimit`, `WithGlobalRateLimit`, `PageFiles`, `PageSHA256`, `Handler.Wait`, `EvaluateGate` and `GateReask`; the key custody guide `awskms.Config.GrantTokens` and `azurekv.Config.HostSuffixes`; the notifications guide the open trail and `WithNotifyUpgradeWindow`; the recovery guide that `RecoverLoop` re-signs runs waiting on a decision; known limitations what `postgres.WithoutMigration` does not check; Concepts the decision receipt; the README `bide migrate`; and the examples index the Azure Key Vault path of `hsm-signing`.
- The viewer and the read-only builder tools show approval deadlines and separation of duties. `bide view` shows a waiting gate's deadline and the time left, an expired gate as `expired` with its deadline, and a decision written after the deadline as not counted (`Gate.ExpiresAt`, `Timeout`, `Expired`, `Overdue`, `Due`; `runview.MarkDeadlines`); the Needs attention list puts gates with a deadline first, nearest first, flags one due within the hour, and marks a gate past its deadline `OVERDUE` with no approve command, since it refuses decisions; `bide approve -list` shows the deadline too. `bide view -config`, `plan`'s `RenderMermaid` (which now labels a gated node with its gate) and `bide check -diff` show each gate's `timeout` and `exclude_principal` next to its need and approvers (`plan.TopologyGate.TimeoutText`), and `-diff` classifies a timeout change: adding or shortening one is strengthened (the gate fails closed sooner), removing or lengthening it is weakened (a run can wait longer, and a late decision counts), and `-fail-on-weaken` exits 1 on the latter.
- `audit.IdentityPolicy` and `CertIdentity.VerifyChain`: the certificate half of the planned certificate-bound approver identity (build step 1, not yet used by the approval gate, so no behaviour changes). Given a decision's leaf-first chain, the time the approver signed and the time the decision became provable, it accepts exactly one chain from the policy's trust anchors through pinned intermediates, with the extended key usage nested along the chain, an algorithm and key profile (RSA 3072 or better, ECDSA P-256 or P-384, Ed25519, ML-DSA-65; never SHA-1), per-key `Distrust` dates, one subject alternative name of the configured kind (an opaque person URI, or an email only by explicit choice) in a strict canonical form, a required certificate policy, and a leaf key whose scheme is derived from the certificate. Certificate policies nest along the chain (a CA that maps policies is refused, and `RequirePolicyOnCAs` optionally refuses an issuing CA with no policies), the leaf's key and extended key usages are exclusive, leaf lifetime and the proof-window tolerances have ceilings, and chains that differ only in certificates for the same keys (a renewed root) are one chain. Failures are `*audit.CertError` carrying one of the new `agent.ReasonNoCert`, `ReasonCertChain`, `ReasonCertIdentity`, `ReasonCertUsage` or `ReasonCertWindow` and the precise cause. Revocation status, the v4 decision bytes and the gate wiring follow in later changes.
- `audit.StatusSnapshot` and `CertIdentity.EvaluateStatus`: the revocation half of the planned certificate-bound approver identity (build step 1, not yet used by the approval gate, so no behaviour changes). A snapshot reads CRLs and refuses what bide cannot read without guessing (issuing distribution points, indirect CRLs, critical extensions it does not process, SHA-1 signatures, a missing CRL number or nextUpdate, duplicate extensions, delta CRLs for now); a snapshot can be bound to its issuers (the signature is checked before the entries are built), is size-limited and evicts its oldest CRLs; evaluating an accepted chain checks every non-anchor certificate against CRLs that verify under its own issuing CA, computes the effective revocation time (the earlier of the invalidity date and the revocation time, retroactive to notBefore for a compromise with no invalidity date, with an optional grace for routine reasons), treats a listing in any CRL of the series as a revocation from its time on, reads absence only from CRLs issued after the decision and no later than the first CRL beyond notAfter, ignores status signed by a distrusted key, and reports a tier (no information, provisional, final: status reaching A plus the window over time coverage from the CRL in force at A, with L(C) needed only once the status falls after notAfter, and coverage shown by the CA's declared `CAPeriod` rather than by nextUpdate) with the window, the CRLs relied on and the caveat that later reports are not covered. New `agent.ReasonRevoked`, `ReasonStatusUnknown` and `ReasonStatusStale`. A finding is verified only when `IsFinal()`; its `Basis` says what it rests on, and a live count (`CountLive`, never implied by `Now`) is provisional at most. A snapshot can be unbounded for audits and counts its evictions.
- Each release now signs every SBOM and publishes one more. Each archive's SPDX SBOM gets its own keyless cosign signature (`<archive>.sbom.json.sigstore.json`), and `bide_X.Y.Z_modules.sbom.json` (with its `.sigstore.json`) is the SBOM of the published Go module set: what an application gets with `go get`, built by the new `scripts/module-sbom.sh` from the release's consumer module (the core, every library, the commands as tools) resolved only through the public Go proxy, and checked to list every published bide module at the release's version. The release workflow's `verify-signatures` job verifies each per-archive SBOM's signature, and a new `verify-module-sbom` job verifies the module set SBOM's. `docs/trust/verify-releases.md` shows how to verify the SBOMs and use them (grype, a CycloneDX conversion for Dependency-Track). The actions in the release jobs that can sign (cosign-installer, the syft installer and goreleaser-action) are pinned by commit SHA.
- A retention and archiving guide, `docs/guides/retention.md`, for the people who set a bank's retention policy and the operators who carry it out: the questions a policy has to answer, what each artifact is (journals, evidence packages, signed tree heads and anchor entries, decision receipts, notification trails including the open trail's segments, seals, checkpoints and yearly indexes, relay state, keys) and which of them to keep, how to archive them so `bide audit` still verifies them offline years later (a manifest, the pinned verifier and the formats it reads, what each verb needs), how to rotate and retire keys without breaking old verification, what deleting does against the position-based detection of deletion (checked against a real journal: whole runs can be removed consistently, records and open-trail segments cannot, and bide ships no redaction), and storage on write-once media. It names no regulation as satisfied, and points to the approver-identity design (#302) for certificate and revocation-list archiving, which is not implemented. Linked from the trust pages, the docs index and the docs-site sidebar.
- New package `audit/rfc3161`: a strict, standard-library-only verifier for RFC 3161 time-stamp tokens, the independent time source that the approver identity design requires for gates above the top tier; it reads one narrow DER profile (one `SignerInfo`, SHA-256 or better, ECDSA P-256/P-384, RSA of 3072 bits or more, or Ed25519, the `contentType`, `messageDigest` and `signingCertificateV2` signed attributes, a critical time-stamping-only TSA certificate chained to the auditor's anchors at `genTime`) and refuses everything else with a named `Reason`, checked against OpenSSL `ts` and DigiCert tokens, differentially against `openssl ts -verify`, with a negative vector per refusal, a fuzz target and a fixed mutation budget; it is not called by any command or gate yet, and tokens from DigiCert, GlobalSign, QuoVadis, SSL.com, Microsoft and SwissSign verify by default, tokens from Sectigo, Entrust and FreeTSA (SHA-1 `signingCertificate` only) verify only with the opt-in, off-by-default per-TSA `Policy.AllowESSV1` (the result is labelled `ESSV1`), and Apple's (SHA-1 digest algorithm) never.

### Changed

- Documentation catch-up for the unreleased approval deadlines and gate diff work. `docs/GUARANTEE.md` now states the expiry rule (a gate with a `Timeout` reads its request time and the run's clock; the expiry is insert-once like a pass, so whichever outcome is recorded first stands; an expired gate never runs its call; a decision after the deadline does not count; a recorded deadline is not moved by a redeploy), `docs/CONCEPTS.md` gains an approval deadline entry, the tour and its four translations gain a deadlines paragraph and the weakened-policy list for `bide check -diff`, the translated READMEs match the English run viewer entry, the refund recipe and getting started mention deadlines, `SECURITY.md` mentions the signed SBOMs, and `examples/approval/flow` sets `timeout: 48h` on its gate (with a test that the deadline is recorded). The godoc of `audit/rfc3161` and `audit.IdentityPolicy` now says no gate uses them yet.
- The four translated READMEs now carry what the English README has: the Run viewer entry with its Needs attention list, the paragraph on deciding from a terminal and checking that people were told (with decision receipts and `bide audit verify-receipts`), and the CLI reference and module structure links; and the English tour and its four translations have a stop each for separation of duties (`ExcludePrincipal`), `bide approve` and notifier routing and escalation, scoped like the guides they link. Documentation only.
- `agent.Notifier` is now documented to be called from several goroutines at once, and a custom Notifier must be safe for concurrent use. The engine has always run the rounds of different events concurrently (up to eight at a time, and an event alongside the steps of its escalation chain), so a Notifier that keeps an unguarded counter, map or buffer has a data race; check yours and guard its state with a mutex or atomics. One event still has at most one call in flight, except that a call abandoned at its time limit may still be running when the next is made. bide's own notifiers (`notify.Webhook`, the relay's Slack and PagerDuty sinks) hold no per-call state and are tested under the race detector with concurrent calls.
- The run viewer guide's expired-gate screenshot now shows the approvers and the timeline of a gate that expired while a second approval, stamped before the deadline, was written after the expiry, which the page labels "not counted: written after the gate expired"; `check -diff -check` has a light screenshot beside the dark one, embedded in the run viewer and plan flows guides with `<picture>`. `assets/demo/cli.sh` takes `THEME=light` and `ONLY=<shot>` and writes optimized PNGs, and `assets/demo/setup/deadline.go` writes the late-written decision.

### Fixed

- The approve page's DOM tests (approvals/page/test) no longer sleep a fixed 200 ms for the page to load the call: they wait until the page shows the call or says why not. On a slow Windows runner the fixed sleep ended before the page had rendered, which failed the check that every argument is on display once in CI; the page itself was not at fault and ships unchanged.
- `bide check -diff` closes four gaps in how it compares `assert` lines. An assert line that held and now holds only vacuously (no terminal, or no path to its node, as when a new edge makes the flow cyclic) is reported as weakened; `plan.AssertionResult` gains `Vacuous` (set for such a hold, with a message saying why), and `bide view -config` shows `holds vacuously`. A line written twice is one assertion, with one weakened entry and one change line. Respelling a node name in quotes (or spacing) is no change in any assertion form, where only the approval form was recognised before (`plan.CompareAssertions`; `plan.RenameAssertion` writes a line with its node names renamed and quoted). A node renamed together with its assert line is a rename, not a removed assertion: the line follows the node the diff pairs as renamed, is still decided under the new name, and a rename the check is not sure of (its edges changed too) still reads as removed.
- `bide check -diff` (and `planview.CheckDiff`) now reports weakenings it missed, and `-fail-on-weaken` exits 1 on them. Behaviour change: a node that may have an effect (safety `side_effect`, or not stated) and is reachable without an approval gate, when it was not such a node before, is now a weakening, whether through a new arm around a gate, a gate removed upstream, a raised or dropped `safety`, or as a new node, so a config that leaves `safety` out will see `-fail-on-weaken` fail on new or changed unknown-safety nodes; state `safety: readonly` where it holds (the example refund flows now do). Also reported: an `assert` line removed or loosened (an approval assertion with a lower K, a larger N, or without `excluding the requester`; a tightened one is a strengthening); a Switch arm condition changed beside an arm that leads to an ungated effect (predicates are not readable, so it asks for a review); and, without a `plan.Registry`, a block changed on an ungated effect node. A node renamed with the same block, safety and edges is reported as `renamed` (with its gate change, if any), not as a removed node and a new one. `DiffView` and each `Change` gain a `weakening` field (`gate`, `path`, `block`, `condition` or `assertion`), the page's verdict reads `SAFETY CHECK WEAKENED` when no gate policy itself was weakened, and `plan` gains `CompareAssertions` and `ConfigReport.UngatedPath`.
- `bide check -diff` (and `planview.CheckDiff`) now reports an `assert` line that the new config keeps as it was but that no longer holds as a weakening: a line that held in the old config and is now violated (with the counterexample path), cannot be decided, or no longer reads makes `-fail-on-weaken` exit 1 and the page read SAFETY CHECK WEAKENED. Before, only a removed or loosened line was reported, so a new arm around a gate left `every path to charge passes approval >= 2 of 3` as written and false, and the diff said nothing. A line that did not hold before is not reported.
- `bide check -diff -fail-on-weaken` (and `planview.CheckDiff`) now exits 1 when a change makes a node reachable around an approval gate whatever the node's `safety`: any node that every path reached through an approval gate before, and that some path now reaches without one, is reported as weakened, with the node, its safety and the shortest ungated path. Before, only nodes labeled `side_effect` or not stated counted, so an `idempotent` node that moves money, or the same edit relabeling the node `readonly` and adding the bypass, exited 0. A `safety` moved toward read-only (side effect or unknown to idempotent or read-only, idempotent to read-only) is itself reported as weakened (`Change.Weakening` gains `safety`), the old label counts as well as the new one in the effect checks (the new-effect rule and the Switch-arm rule), and a node renamed together with a safety change is still paired as the same node and judged on both; a new node that shares a removed node's block and kind is also judged against it, and a new node reachable without a gate that uses a block every node used only behind a gate before is reported whatever its name and label. A configuration that wires a read-only node in around a gate will now see `-fail-on-weaken` fail where it did not.
- `doccheck` now checks that every relative link in `README.md`, `examples/README.md` and `docs/` names a file that exists and, with a `#fragment`, a heading that exists (`doccheck -links` runs only this, on every pull request including documentation-only ones, because the docs site sets `ignoreDeadLinks`). It found two broken links, in the notifications and approval guides, now fixed. The trust page on stability now names evidence format `bide.audit.evidence.v6` (it said v5), states that a v5 package still verifies, and lists the decision receipt and notification trail export tags; the v0.17.0 release notes no longer say the approval intake requires `WithAuth` (only `WithPage` and `WithCORS` do), `approvals.New`'s godoc says that `WithGatePolicy` is required, and the docs index and README link the `bide-hsm` reference and list every `bide` command.
- A notification call that had returned could still be counted as in flight for a moment, so a retry made at once in the same round was refused as "an earlier call was abandoned" although nothing was abandoned. The attempt was lost: the sink saw one call fewer than the round made, and a sink that fails a fixed number of calls then failed a later, unrelated notification instead (seen on Windows, where it made a test see an all-clear delivered twice, the first attempt having failed). The engine now records that a call is over before it hands the answer back, so the next call is never refused spuriously; a call that really is stuck is still not repeated until it returns.
- `TestStartFailureStopsTheCommand` (the `bide sample` test helper) could fail on a loaded runner with "1 bide sample command(s) still running after the failed start": its running count was decremented by a deferred call that runs after the exit status is sent, so the test could read the count in between. The count now drops before the status is sent. Test code only.
- The `bide sample` tests (`TestServe`, `TestLive`) waited 20 seconds for the viewer to come up, which a loaded Windows runner did not always meet, and on that failure left `bide sample` running over the test directory, so its cleanup failed with a sharing violation on `sample.db`. They now wait up to two minutes for the printed address, and every path stops the command and waits for it to return before the directory is removed; a test covers the failed start.
- The SQLite store's and the shared store suite's timing assumptions no longer break on a slow disk: the lease-under-load test sizes its TTL from the commit time it measures and tells a disk stall from a starved renewal, the suite's "writes inside a Runs loop" deadline scales with the store's measured write cost (a shared Windows runner took 30ms and more per commit, which exceeded the fixed one minute), and `docs/KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md` and the `AcquireLease` godoc now say that a lease TTL must exceed the disk's worst commit latency, since a renewal is a committed write that cannot finish faster than the disk.
- Two places released a resource after handing the result to a caller that could act on it at once. `audit.AuditedEventStore` held a trail's slot until a deferred release ran after the head's answer was sent, so a caller that published the next head right away could find the slot taken; the slot is now released before the answer. `agent.RecoverLoop` called the `WithRecoverErrors` handler while the failing run still held its slot and in-flight mark, so a handler that waited on other work (with a small `WithRecoverConcurrency`) stalled it; the handler now runs after both are released. A new repository check, `internal/tools/sendcheck`, fails the Lint job when a goroutine sends or closes a result and only then runs a deferred decrement, release or unlock, with an allowlist for the orders that are safe, each with its reason. A flush of the notifier (`FlushNotifications`, `RunNotifier.Flush`) called from inside the `WithNotifyErrors` handler used to wait for the round that was calling the handler, a deadlock; it now returns an error wrapping `ErrConfig` at once, and the `WithNotifyErrors` godoc and the notifications guide say the handler must be short and must not flush.
- `bide check`, `bide view -config` and the YAML flow of a `bide init` project now refuse a YAML flow config that holds more than one document (error `more than one YAML document`), where the converter read the first document and silently ignored the rest, so a second flow or policy in the same file was read by no check and no loader; the JSON path already refused data after the value. This is a behaviour change: a multi-document YAML flow file that was accepted is now an error, and a leading `---` with a closing `...` is still one document. Programs that convert YAML themselves (as the scaffold did) should decode with a `yaml.Decoder` and require the next decode to return `io.EOF`.

## [0.17.0] - 2026-10-15

### Added

- `agent.EvaluateGate` says where an approval gate stands on a call (waiting, decided, single, ungated, or misconfigured, with the tally, the classification of each decision and why a gate asks again), reading the journal, the live policy and the approver verifiers exactly as the agent's own gate does: the gate's counting is now one function (`countGate`) used by both, and `agent.GateReask` is the rule for a recorded tally the gate supersedes. `bide view` uses `GateReask`. No behavior of the gate changes.
- Separation of duties for m-of-n approval gates: `agent.ApprovalPolicy.ExcludePrincipal` (a flow config's `approval: {..., exclude_principal: true}`) makes the gate never count a decision by a requester of the run, its journaled principal (`Identity.OnBehalfOf`) or that of any run above it (a sub-agent's, a delegation's, a flow started from a tool), compared ignoring ASCII case, with `agent.ReasonRequester`; the tally records `ExcludePrincipal`, `Requesters` and the excluded approvers in `Excluded`, and the gate refuses with `ErrConfig` a run with no requester or an ancestor that records none, a requester that is not printable ASCII, a principal journaled after the call's request (a run an earlier version started), or a policy whose `Need` is out of reach without the requesters; a passing tally recorded without the exclusion does not decide a gate whose policy now excludes the requester (it asks again, `agent.PendingSoDReapproval`, and records `agent.ApprovalSoDRetallyStep`), and a recorded excluding tally must name the run's requesters. A run's first drive now records `run:principal` (`agent.RunPrincipal`: its principal and its ancestors', nothing of its input) before `run:start`, which also records the ancestors (`RunStart.Ancestors`); `plan.Flow.Run` now takes `agent.WithIdentity` (any other run option is `ErrConfig`), and a plan flow run journals the principal of that identity, or of the one its context carries, and a later flow drive under another principal is `ErrConfig` (a flow run recorded with none, as every earlier one was, keeps none). `audit.ApprovalEvidence` discloses the run's `run:principal` (not its `run:start`) for such a gate, `audit.VerifyApprovals` recounts with the same exclusion (`ApprovalVerdict.Requesters`), `bide audit verify-approvals` and `bide view` take `-exclude-principal`, `bide approve` refuses a requester's decision and counts under the exclusion, `bide view` marks a requester's card as excluded, the approval notification lists the excluded approvers (`approval.excluded`), and a config diff reports dropping the exclusion as a weakened gate, the flow assertion `every path to X passes approval >= K of N excluding the requester` checks it on the graph, model 1b checks `SeparationOfDuties` up a delegation chain, and `examples/approval/makerchecker` runs a maker-checker refund. New exported API: `agent.RunPrincipal`, `agent.ApprovalSoDRetallyStep`, `agent.SupersededTallies`, `agent.PendingSoDReapproval`, `agent.SameApprover`, `agent.NotifyApproval.Excluded`, `agent.RecordedPrincipal`, `agent.IsRunPrincipal`, `agent.ReasonRequester`, `agent.ReasonNoRequester`, `plan.TopologyGate.ExcludePrincipal`.
- A gate that asks again over a recorded tally it does not act on (one that predates `bide.approval.v3`, or one that passed without excluding the requester under a policy that now excludes it) records a re-ask note, `approval-pending:<id>:v3` or `:sod` (`agent.ApprovalReaskStep`): an `agent.ApprovalWait` with the policy it asks under, why (`Reask`) and the tally it asks over (`Supersedes`), written only if absent by every drive that pauses at the re-ask. `bide view`, `bide approve`, the approvals intake's note fallback, notifications and `audit.ApprovalEvidence` read the newest note (`agent.LatestPauseNote`), so after a redeploy adds `ExcludePrincipal` and a crash leaves a passing tally under the old policy, the CLI and the viewer show the gate waiting for the re-approval and take the approvals it needs. A re-ask notifies as an event of its own (`agent.ApprovalReaskKey`, `NotifyApproval.Reask`, `reask` in the webhook payload), `audit.ApprovalVerdict` gains `Reask` and `ReaskedAt`, and `plan.Flow.Conform` accepts re-ask notes. A re-ask note counts only where the journal proves it (`agent.ValidReaskNote`: a recorded tally the gate would ask again over, which the note names), so a note pre-inserted by a journal writer without approver keys is ignored by every reader, and the notifier's catch-up scan, escalations and `agent.JournalNotifyEvents` (so `audit.VerifyNotifications`) keep the re-ask's own key and question. The record is additive: earlier readers ignore it, except an earlier `Conform`, which reports it as unexpected.
- The approval intake has a browser page: an approver opens a link from a notification, sees the call, and signs a decision with an Ed25519 key held non-extractable in the browser (WebCrypto, IndexedDB). The page computes the SHA-256 of the canonical arguments itself from what it displays and signs that, signs nothing if the call or its gate changes between the display and the signature, and checks the receipt the endpoint returns. `approvals.PageFiles` and `go run github.com/bide-ai/bide/approvals/cmd/approvepage` give a deterministic bundle with published hashes for an organization to host on an origin of its own; `approvals.WithCORS` lets that origin call the intake (the preflight is answered before authentication); `approvals.WithPage` serves it from the application for development and says so on its face. Both need `WithAuth`. The canonical-arguments encoding and the decision bytes have golden vectors generated by Go (`agent/testdata/canonical_args_vectors.json`) that the page's script is tested against. The page shows every argument (none is clipped), shows a re-asked gate's reason, and offers no decision on a gate the endpoint reports as `error`, `single` or `decided`; its requests carry the page origin's credentials, and `config.json` can ask for `include` with `approvals.WithCORSCredentials` for a cookie or a client certificate cross-origin; the CORS preflight is rate limited and answered without waiting for a body; the browser's CSP block of a changed `config.json` intake is reported. Passkeys (WebAuthn) are a separate, later design; gates for high-value actions should wait for them. A CI job (Page mTLS (Chrome)) drives the page in real Chrome on Linux with a TLS client certificate, same-origin and cross-origin with credentials include (approvals/page_mtls_test.go).
- Package `approvals` is an `http.Handler` an application mounts to take approvers' signed decisions over HTTP: `GET /pending` shows a gated call from the journal (tool, arguments, the arguments' SHA-256, the gate), and `POST /decisions` verifies a signed decision against the recorded call and the approver's enrolled key, then records it through the application's own journal, so an `audit.AuditedStore` signs it as it is written, and answers the gate's standing (from the gate's recorded tally once it has settled, with separation of duties honored), whether the decision counts, and its receipt. `WithGatePolicy` is required and gives the handler each tool's live policy; the handler evaluates the gate with the new `agent.EvaluateGate`, the function the agent's own gate evaluates with (a gate the gate would refuse is state `error` and `409 gate_misconfigured`, a one-decision gate is state `single`), so a policy changed after the first pause (`ExcludePrincipal` turned on) is honored and the new approvals are not refused as `not_waiting`. A gate that asks again (its recorded tally passed on decisions of an earlier format, or without excluding a requester the policy now excludes) is `waiting`, not decided. Every response sent without reading a request body (a refusal, a 404 or 405, a GET that carries one) closes the connection instead of waiting for the body, and every path, those that route to nothing included, is rate limited; IPv6 clients are limited by their /64; the authorization hook's two calls for a POST are told apart by `Access.Phase`. A POST is authorized from its headers before its body is read, the body is read in a bounded pool under a deadline, and only then does the request take a slot for work on the journal; requests are strict JSON, size-bounded, rate limited per client and overall, and capped in concurrent work; `WithAuth` takes a mutual-TLS or identity check; a replay is a no-op; `OnRecorded` runs once for the request that wrote the decision and whose record the gate counts (a randomized signature can journal one decision twice under a racing retry; the duplicate is superseded), to re-run the run. It adds `agent.DecisionStep` (the journal name of a decision), `agent.RootRunID`, and `agent.ReportWritten` (an option for `SubmitDecision` that says whether this call's insert stored the record, so of concurrent identical submissions exactly one acts). Single-approver gates and plan flow node gates are refused.
- `audit.DecisionReceipt` (format `bide.audit.decision-receipt.v1`, `audit.ReceiptFormat`) is the signed decision an approver keeps, with the journal record's name and position and when it was recorded, and no key material: `NewDecisionReceipt` makes one, `Verify` checks it on its own, and `audit.CheckReceipt` decides whether the signed tree heads of the journal commit to the decision, as `anchored`, `redacted (anchored)`, `pending`, `receipt not matched by the journal`, `decision deleted after signing` (only for a receipt with an anchoring proof, `AnchorReceipt`) or `unchecked`, by the receipt's journal position and never by time. `bide audit verify-receipts` runs it from files and, with `-update`, stores the anchoring proof in each receipt, and `bide approve` writes a receipt for each signed decision (`-receipt`) and checks one with `-verify-receipt`.
- `audit.ErrRootMismatch` is a new sentinel wrapped by the error of a proof whose signed tree head's root is not the root of the run's journal (the message is unchanged); `bide demo` uses it to explain a failed verification in plain words.
- `bide-hsm` takes an `azurekv:https://<vault>.vault.azure.net/keys/<name>/<32-hex-version>` key reference for an approver key in Azure Key Vault or Managed HSM: the version is pinned (a base identifier, a port, credentials, a query, a fragment, an upper-case host or a host outside the Azure public, China and US Government Key Vault suffixes is refused, and the error repeats no value from the reference), the key is ECDSA P-256 (`-alg ed25519` is refused), only an HSM-protected key is used unless `-allow-software-key`, and credentials are the Azure SDK's default chain. Tested against the Azure SDK's fake Key Vault server in the test-only module `cmd/bide/bide-hsm/azuretest`, never against Azure.
- `bide-hsm` takes a `gcpkms:projects/<p>/locations/<l>/keyRings/<r>/cryptoKeys/<k>/cryptoKeyVersions/<n>` key reference for an approver key in Google Cloud KMS: the numeric version is pinned (an alias, `latest`, a query or a malformed name is refused, and the error repeats no value from the reference), credentials are Google's application default credentials, only an HSM-backed version is used unless `-allow-software-key` (Cloud KMS has no HSM-backed Ed25519 key, and `bide-hsm approve` says beside the key when it is at protection level SOFTWARE), and `-verify-attestation` requires the version's Cloud HSM attestation. Tested against gcp-kms-emulator v0.10.0 in the test process (in the test-only module `cmd/bide/bide-hsm/emulatortest`, which keeps the emulator out of bide-hsm's module graph), never against Google Cloud.
- `bide-hsm`, a companion to `bide approve` for approvers whose signing keys are in an HSM, on a smart card or token, or in AWS KMS: `bide-hsm approve` is the same command (one implementation of the display, confirmation, signature, record and receipt) with `-key` naming the key by an RFC 7512 `pkcs11:` URI or an `awskms:` key ARN, and `bide-hsm pubkey` prints the `-approver-keys` entry that enrols it. References are parsed strictly (a PIN in the URI, an unknown attribute or a repeated one is refused, and no error repeats a value from the reference), the PIN comes from a file, an environment variable or a prompt on the controlling terminal (never a pipe), the token's own PIN pad (`pin-source=protected`), and a PKCS#11 key the token does not mark non-extractable is refused unless `-allow-extractable`. It is its own module under `cmd/bide` and needs cgo for PKCS#11 keys, so the `bide` binary stays pure Go and reproducible; it is installed from source (`go install github.com/bide-ai/bide/cmd/bide/bide-hsm@latest`), not in the release archives, and is tested against SoftHSM2 and a fake KMS client only.
- `bide approve` records your signed approval, or with `-reject` a rejection, of a call that waits at an approval gate: it reads the run, tool, arguments and gate from the journal (SQLite `-db`, or Postgres by `-postgres-env`), shows the SHA-256 of the arguments the signature binds, asks you to confirm (it will not sign arguments longer than its view unless `-full` shows them all), signs with your Ed25519 key and records the decision with `agent.SubmitDecision` (`agent.Approve` for a single-approver gate, unsigned). It refuses a call with no waiting gate, a gate already decided, an approver not in the policy, an approver who already decided, and on Unix a key file readable by group or others; an m-of-n decision requires `-approver-keys` (the enrolled public keys, the file `bide view` reads), refuses a key that is not the one enrolled and verifies the tally, and `-unverified` opts out with a warning and an unverified tally. `bide approve -list` lists the waiting calls with the command that decides each, and `bide keygen` creates an approver key pair (mode 0600, never overwritten; `-alg` picks `ed25519`, `ml-dsa-65` or the `ed25519+ml-dsa-65` hybrid) and prints the public key policies and verifiers read. Both commands hold the key as an `audit.Signer`.
- `bide approve -to <url>` (and `bide-hsm approve -to`) decides through an application's approval intake (`approvals.Handler`) instead of writing a journal: it fetches the pending call from the endpoint, shows it, signs the digest of the arguments it computed from what it showed (and refuses an endpoint whose own digest differs, or whose tool, arguments or gate differ after the confirmation), and posts the decision, so the application records it through its own journal and its audit log signs it as it is written. A decision the endpoint says does not count is a failure, as in the local flow. The URL must be https (http only to loopback), a redirect is not followed, and `-tls-ca`, `-client-cert` with `-client-key`, and `-token-env` cover a private CA, mutual TLS and a bearer token. The endpoint returns the decision's receipt, which the command checks against what it signed, and refuses if it carries an anchoring proof, before writing it.
- `audit` signs and verifies with ECDSA P-256 (`ecdsa-p256`, `audit.ECDSAP256Signer`) beside Ed25519, ML-DSA-65 and the hybrid, for the HSMs and cloud KMS (AWS KMS, Azure Key Vault, PKCS#11) that cannot sign Ed25519: SHA-256 over the message, a 64-byte low-S `r || s` signature (a high-S or non-canonical one is refused), and a 65-byte uncompressed SEC1 public key written `ecdsa-p256:<hex>`, with `ECDSAP256SignatureFromDER`, `ECDSAP256SignatureFromRaw` and `ECDSAP256PublicKeyFromSPKI` for adapters; it works for approval decisions, tree heads, evidence, run certificates and grants, in the standalone `audit/verify` package and in every `-pubkey` and `-approver-keys` flag of `bide audit` and `bide view`. P-256 is not a hybrid half, and hybrid post-quantum keys inside an HSM stay deferred. No format changes for this: the scheme is a string in each artifact, and an older verifier refuses an `ecdsa-p256` artifact as malformed.
- The records that resolve something a person was told about now note when they were written (`recorded_at`, `Record.RecordedAt`, canonical RFC 3339 UTC to the millisecond): a single approver's decision (`agent.Approve`, which now takes `WithClock`), an interrupt answer and a signal (`AnswerInterrupt` and `Signal`, which take `WithClock` too), the result `agent.ResolveHalt` injects (the resolver signs nothing, so the time is the writing process's unsigned claim), a run's `run:complete`, `run:aborted` and `run:cancelled` markers and a saga's `run:cancel-requested` (`agent.Cancel` now takes `WithClock`; `agent.Status` reports `RunStatus.EndedAt`). The clock is `WithClock`, else the run's, else the wall clock; the first write sets the time. It is informational like `started_at`: no gate, claim, replay or recovery pass reads it, the journal format tag is unchanged, a record from an earlier version has none and is reported as having none, and a clock with no canonical form records none. `bide view` places these records on the timeline and shows the end and resolution times in the verdict, the `ended_at` and event `recorded_at` in its JSON view; `bide audit verify` prints a proven record's time and `bide audit verify-evidence` adds it to a tool call's or step's line. `bide audit notifications` now judges an all-clear owed for a single-approver gate or a `ResolveHalt`-resolved halt against `-grace` from that record's time, so `all_clear_untimed` is reported only for a journal an earlier version wrote; `agent.JournalNotifyEvents` returns a `ResolveHalt`-resolved halt as an event that is no longer current. Behaviour change: a `ResolveHalt`-resolved halt that the trail never observed is now reported as the warning `unobserved_resolved`, so `bide audit notifications -strict` can fail on journals that passed before.
- `bide keygen -alg ecdsa-p256` creates an ECDSA P-256 approver key (the file holds `ecdsa-p256:` and the 32-byte private scalar as hex, mode 0600), and `bide approve` signs with it, so an approver can use the scheme their HSM or cloud KMS signs; a key file whose scalar is zero or not below the group order is refused without echoing it.
- The notifier now sends an all-clear. When an `outcome_unknown`, `approval_pending` or `recovery_failing` event that a receiver was, or may have been, told of stops being current (the halt was resolved, the gate decided, the run ended, recovery recovered), a `resolved` notification (`agent.NotificationResolved`) is delivered with the same audited claim and acknowledgement trail, at-least-once delivery, stable ID and retry as any event, so a PagerDuty incident, ticket or Slack thread can be closed. It names the event it ends in `Notification.Clears` (`Key`, `ID`, `Kind`) and the reason in `Notification.Reason`; in the webhook payload that is a `clears` object (`id`, `key`, `kind`, null on every other kind) and the existing `reason` field, and `deadline_at` is null. The ID of the event is final: a receiver treats every delivery of that ID, before or after the all-clear, as superseded, which is how an all-clear that arrives first is matched. It is never overdue (no deadline, no overdue notice) and is retried like any delivery. An event never claimed, and `run_aborted`, get none. It is found by a scan of the run's trail after every drive of a root run, every recovery pass and every unfinished run `NotifyPending` lists (one trail read each, off the drive's path), and, for a run that completed while nobody scanned, through the new open trail (`watch` and `unwatch` records, written before an event is observed and when nothing more is owed) that every sweep, `Recover` pass and `RecoverLoop` pass reads; it is kept in daily sealed segments `notify:@open:<date>` with checkpoints and a yearly index, so a read does not grow with history, and the first sweep or recovery pass lists events observed before it existed. A plan flow's run is now its own root on both the hook path and the sweep, so a flow whose ID holds a `>` files its gate under one trail. The `observed` record of a halt or approval gains `op_run`, `op_kind` and `op_id` (additive), so an ID too long to encode in the key can still be checked. Receivers that treated every webhook POST as a new alert should filter on `kind`. TLA+ model 14 gains the all-clear (`Clears`, key `r_<event>`) with its safety and liveness properties, regression configurations and the ordering limit (`limits/clear-before-event`). The open trail is verified for its time: every record is dated in its segment's UTC day, a seal may not skip an indexed segment, an index names segments of its year, and each segment is indexed in the index of its own year even when a writer repairs a gap. An open-trail record written by a process whose clock is behind its segment (another process's clock ran ahead and sealed into a later day) has its time moved later, never earlier, and keeps its own clock reading in a new `clock_at` field, which the verifier checks. During a rolling upgrade, any scan lists an event an older process delivered without listing it, and a second backfill pass after `WithNotifyUpgradeWindow` (a day by default; set it to how long your staged rollout takes) after the first (the first pass records the window in `backfill_window`, the checkpoint carries it with `backfills` and `backfill_at`, and the verifier requires the second pass no earlier than the first plus that window) lists what no scan reached. A panic in a `NotifyTrail` or `Notifier` now fails the round or call instead of the process, and a flush stops waiting for the catch-up scans and the backfill after ten seconds, so a slow trail store cannot hold a `Recover` pass.
- The notifier routes events and escalates the ones nobody handles. `agent.WithNotifyReceiver` names additional receivers (each its own `Notifier`, typically its own webhook and relay), `WithNotifyRoute` sends an event kind to one (approvals to the approvers' channel, halts and recovery failures to on-call), and `WithNotifyApproverRoute` routes an approval by approver ID; a gate whose approvers map to several receivers is delivered to each under its own key (`to:<receiver>:<key>`), so one receiver's acknowledgement never stands for another's. An approver with no route is reached through the route of the kind, which is delivered to as well when any approver of the gate is unrouted. `WithNotifyEscalation` gives `outcome_unknown`, `approval_pending` and `recovery_failing` a chain of up to four steps: when the event is not acknowledged by its deadline (an approval: not decided by it) the next receiver is told in an `escalated` notification (`agent.NotificationEscalated`, `Notification.Escalates`) with a deadline of its own, and the chain stops when the event stops being current or, for an event that is not an approval, is acknowledged; receivers already told get the all-clear. Routing lives in the engine and not in a fan-out `Notifier`, so every receiver has its own audited `observed`, `claimed` and `acked` records, retries and overdue in the run's one trail. The trail gains two additive `observed` fields, `receiver` (empty for the default receiver, so trails written without routing are unchanged) and `level`, the event kind `escalated` and the keys `to:<receiver>:<key>` and `escalated:<level>:<key>`; `NotifyEvent.Receiver`, `NotifyEvent.Level` and `NotifyState.ByReceiver` read them. The webhook payload gains `receiver` and `escalates` (null on every other kind). TLA+ model 14 gains routing (two receivers of one gate) and escalation (`Levels`, `EscMode`) with their safety and liveness properties, and regression configurations for a shared acknowledgement, an early step, a skipped step, a step after an acknowledgement, no escalation and a step that outlives its event.
- `bide notify` sets up and tests the webhook notifier. `bide notify test` sends one signed sample notification to a receiver through the notifier's own code path (payload, signature, TLS 1.2+, private CA, key pin, mutual TLS) and reports the TLS handshake, the HTTP status, whether the receiver acknowledged and the timing, with a next step for each failure; `bide notify listen` is a local HTTPS receiver with a self-signed certificate (it prints the CA file and pin) that verifies each request with `notify.Verify` and prints the event; `bide notify relay` verifies bide's webhooks and forwards them to Slack (a Block Kit message with the run, kind, summary, deadline and a viewer link) and PagerDuty (Events API v2 trigger keyed by the event ID, and a resolve on the all-clear `resolved` kind using its `clears.id`). The relay answers 502 when a forward fails so the notifier retries, drops redeliveries by delivery ID in a bounded cache, retries only the destination that failed, drops a trigger whose event it already saw cleared, never forwards error text or tool arguments, and never logs a secret or a webhook URL. Secrets are read from an environment variable or a file, never a flag. The relay is also a library, package `notify/relay` (`relay.Handler`, `relay.Sink`, `relay.NewSlack`, `relay.NewPagerDuty`), and `notify.Webhook.Probe` reports a delivery's handshake, status and timing for tools that test a receiver.
- The notifier can now sign and anchor its notification trail as it is written: wrap the trail's event store in `audit.NewAuditedEventStore(store, signer, anchor)` (new) and each growth of a run's trail publishes a signed tree head (kind events, run ID the trail's name, the head `audit.SignNotifyTrail` signs) with the same `audit.Signer` and `audit.Anchor` as a journal, so HSM, KMS and P-256 signers work unchanged. Publishing is a background, best-effort side channel: a publish failure or a hung anchor never fails or delays a delivery or an acknowledgement (one attempt is abandoned after `WithTimeout`, default 3s, and a failure pauses publishing for `WithCooldown`, default 30s), failures go to `OnError`, heads only grow per trail, and `Flush`, `Reanchor` and `ReanchorPending` publish what an outage left out. `NotifyPending`, `RecoverLoop`, `Recover` and `FlushNotifications` sign again any trail (the open trail's segments and index and the digest's trail included) that grew past its last head through the new optional `agent.NotifyTrailAnchorer` (implemented by `audit.NotifyTrail`). `bide audit notifications` now takes any number of `-sth` heads per trail (each must verify and match a prefix; the newest is used), reports how many records follow the newest head and how long the oldest has gone unattested, and fails a gap older than the new `-max-unattested`; `audit.NotifyVerifyInput.MaxTailAge` and the `Heads`, `TailSince` and `TailAge` report fields are new. Model 14 is unchanged: anchoring is outside the delivery protocol.
- `bide audit notifications` and `audit.VerifyNotifications` check offline that every event that needed a person was notified and acknowledged, and when. The notification trail is read strictly (an unknown record kind or field is an error, not skipped) and must be a history a notifier writes (an event observed once and first, nothing after its acknowledgement or clearing, an acknowledgement after a claim, in-order times); with a signed tree head over the trail (`audit.SignNotifyTrail`, new, and `audit.ExportNotifyTrail`, format `bide.audit.notify-trail-export.v1`) a deleted, reordered or changed record is found, and a trail with no head is reported `UNANCHORED` (the notifier does not sign its trail; `-unanchored` accepts one). Each event gets a verdict: `acked` with its times, attempts and authentication, `acked_late`, `pending`, `failing`, `overdue`, `cleared_before_notification`, `cleared_unacknowledged`, `never_notified`; the run journals are cross-checked (`agent.JournalNotifyEvents`, new) so an approval gate, unknown-outcome halt or aborted run with no notify record is `never_notified`, and an overdue record without its notice, a long recovery failure episode without its event and a clearing without its all-clear are caught the same way. A gate decided by m-of-n decisions whose all-clear is missing past `-grace` of the newest decision's `recorded_at` is `never_notified`; one whose resolution the journal does not time is the warning `all_clear_untimed`, like `unobserved_resolved` (a decided gate no notifier observed), and `-strict` (library: `Strict`) fails the audit on either. The verb exits 1 on `acked_late`, `overdue` and `never_notified` and takes `-max-ack` as a bound; `-db` and `-postgres-env` (a variable holding the DSN) read every run, or `-run`, from a store in the full `bide` command, and the audit-only build reads exported files.
- `postgres.WithoutMigration` opens the Postgres store (and `New`) without running the schema migration, so the application's database role needs no `CREATE` on the schema: it verifies the schema and refuses a missing one (`postgres.ErrSchemaMissing`) or one at another version (`postgres.ErrSchemaVersion`) without creating or altering anything. `postgres.Migrate` and `MigrateDB` run the migration on its own and report the schema version before and after, what they created and the SQL (`postgres.WithDryRun` rolls it back), and the new `bide migrate -postgres-env VAR [-schema S] [-dry-run]` command runs it as the owner role with the DSN read from the environment. `OpenReadOnly` now returns the same typed errors. Every way of opening the store, and `Migrate` after migrating, now also checks each table's columns (type, NOT NULL, a deterministic collation), the lease indexes' definitions, that no generated or identity column, extra unique index or CHECK, EXCLUDE or foreign-key constraint, trigger, rule, row-level security, partition or inheritance affects the tables (each must be an ordinary table), in both Postgres modules, and refuses a schema that differs (`agent.ErrConfig`); a database that was altered that way is now refused at open. `bide migrate` requires `-schema` and redacts a URL or keyword-form DSN from errors. The Postgres store guide (`docs/guides/postgres.md`) documents the owner, application and reader roles; the governed log has the same split (`postgreslog.WithoutMigration`, `postgreslog.Migrate`, `postgreslog.ErrSchemaMissing`), and the log now records a schema version in `governed_events_schema_version` (a log without the table is version 1, and `Migrate` or a migrating `Open` adds it) and refuses another with `postgreslog.ErrSchemaVersion`. `bide migrate`, `bide approve` and `bide doctor` now refuse a `-postgres-env` value that is not an environment variable name (a DSN given by mistake) as a usage error that does not echo it, via the new `approveline.ValidEnvName`.
- `agent.RecoverLoop` and `agent.Recover` re-sign a run whose drive ended waiting on an approval or a halt resolution, including each of several joined pauses from parallel sub-agents (`agent.Reanchorer`, implemented by `audit.AuditedStore`, found through `Unwrap`; `agent.WithReanchor` names another function or turns it off; `agent.WithReanchorTimeout`, half the lease TTL by default, bounds each call so a hung anchor cannot hold a lease and a slot), so a decision another process wrote straight into the database is under a signed tree head within one pass instead of at the application's next write to the run. `audit.CheckDecisionCoverage` and `bide audit verify-decision-coverage` check a journal export against signed heads and flag a decision no head covers past a bound stated in journal positions (`-max-records`) or, as the weaker basis, age (`-max-age`, under which a decision with no recorded time is over the bound); a head the log key signed for the run that does not match the export is divergent and fails the check, so a deleted or truncated decision cannot pass.
- Each release publishes the approve page bundle's SHA-256 sums, `approve-page.SHA256SUMS` (the `SHA256SUMS` that `go run ./approvals/cmd/approvepage -out <dir>` writes), signed keylessly with cosign like `checksums.txt` (`approve-page.SHA256SUMS.sigstore.json`), because Subresource Integrity pins the page's script and stylesheet but cannot pin its entry document. The release workflow builds the bundle from the tag and checks it, the `verify-signatures` job verifies the signature, and the `repro` job rebuilds the bundle on macOS and fails unless its sums equal the published ones. `docs/trust/verify-releases.md` shows how an organization that hosts the page checks the files it serves against them.
- Audit-log and approver signing keys can live in Azure Key Vault or Managed HSM: the new module `signer/azurekv` signs ECDSA P-256 only (Key Vault has no Ed25519), sending the SHA-256 digest with `ES256`, folding the service's 64-byte `r||s` to the low-S form audit verifies and checking every signature against the key's public key, with one self-test signature at open. It pins a full key identifier with its version (a version-less identifier is refused) and refuses a key that is disabled, expired, not yet valid, without the `sign` operation, exportable or on another curve; an HSM-protected key (`EC-HSM`, or any Managed HSM key) is required unless `Config.AllowSoftwareKey` is set. Credentials come from `azidentity` only, and errors carry only the operation, HTTP status and Azure error code, never a response body or token. It is tested against a fake client and the Azure SDK's in-process fake server, never against Azure; `examples/hsm-signing` gains `BIDE_AZURE_KEY_ID`. See the key custody guide.
- `gcpkms.Config.VerifyAttestation` makes the Google Cloud KMS signer verify the Cloud HSM attestation of its key version at `Open`, following Google's reference verifier: the manufacturer (Marvell) and owner (Google) certificate chains must build from pinned roots, both partition certificates must verify the attestation signature, and one attested EC object must carry the public key `GetPublicKey` returned, be non-extractable and generated on the HSM, and name the key version; otherwise `Open` fails with `gcpkms.ErrAttestation`. The roots are built in with pinned fingerprints and replaceable through `Config.AttestationRoots`; certificate validity periods are not enforced (Marvell's root has expired), the parser is bounds-checked and size-limited, and the check is off by default and needs an HSM key version. It is tested against the real attestation, chain and public key of a Cloud HSM P-256 key version, a real version 1 chain, and synthetic attestations and chains, and not against a live key service call.
- The new module `signer/gcpkms` is an `audit.Signer` whose Ed25519 (`EC_SIGN_ED25519`) or ECDSA P-256 (`EC_SIGN_P256_SHA256`) key lives in Google Cloud KMS, selected by `Config.Scheme` (default P-256, the scheme Cloud KMS offers in an HSM) like the PKCS#11 and AWS KMS signers: it pins a full `CryptoKeyVersion` name (no "latest" alias), refuses a version of the other algorithm or one that is not `ENABLED`, is fail-closed about protection level (only `HSM` and `HSM_SINGLE_TENANT` open by default; `Config.AllowSoftwareKey` and `Config.AllowExternalKey` opt out, and because Google offers Ed25519 in software only the Ed25519 scheme needs `AllowSoftwareKey`, so a bank that needs an HSM key uses P-256), follows Google's data integrity guide (request and response CRC32C checks, a signature checksum mismatch retried up to three times), signs once at `Open`, verifies every signature against the public key (`Config.Timeout` bounds each `Sign`, retries included), and reduces a Cloud KMS client error to its gRPC status code (`gcpkms.CodeError`) so no credential text reaches an error. It is tested through the real Cloud KMS Go client against gcp-kms-emulator v0.10.0 running in the test process (no Docker, no credentials; the separate test-only module `signer/gcpkms/emulatortest`) and a fake client for the failures the emulator cannot produce, never against Google Cloud itself; `examples/hsm-signing` takes `BIDE_GCPKMS_KEY_VERSION` to use it, and the key custody guide gains a Google Cloud KMS section with the custom IAM role.
- Signing keys can live in an HSM or a cloud key service behind `audit.Signer`: the new modules `signer/pkcs11` (Ed25519 through PKCS#11 `CKM_EDDSA`, tested against SoftHSM2, PIN from an environment variable or a file, never an argument, with `pkcs11test` for SoftHSM2-backed tests) and `signer/awskms` (Ed25519 through AWS KMS `ECC_NIST_EDWARDS25519`, tested against a fake client) sign tree heads, evidence seals, grants and approver decisions without the private key entering the application (`pkcs11.Config.RequireNonExtractable` makes the token confirm the key is non-extractable), and each signer signs once at open and checks every signature against the key's public key. Both refuse a token or key that cannot do Ed25519 instead of substituting another algorithm. KMS signs at most 4096 bytes, so a large evidence package cannot be sealed with it. The core module gains no cgo and no cloud SDK; see the key custody guide and `examples/hsm-signing`.
- The PKCS#11 and AWS KMS signers take `Config.Scheme`, `audit.AlgEd25519` (the default) or `audit.AlgECDSAP256`, for HSMs and key services that cannot sign Ed25519: PKCS#11 signs the SHA-256 of the message with `CKM_ECDSA` on a P-256 key, and KMS signs the digest with `ECDSA_SHA_256` on an `ECC_NIST_P256` key; both return bide's canonical low-S `r||s`, and a key of the other scheme is refused (`ErrNoP256`, `ErrNoEd25519`) instead of used under the wrong one. Under P-256 KMS has no message-size limit. `pkcs11.CKRError` and the new P-256 tests are covered in the key custody guide.
- Trust documentation for security and risk reviewers in `docs/trust/`: a threat model (assets, actors, trust boundaries, and for each threat what bide prevents, detects and does not address), stability and support (the 0.x versioning policy, format versions, what stays verifiable across versions, deprecation), a comparison with Temporal, LangGraph and retries with idempotency keys, operations guidance (key custody, journal retention and archiving, backups, clocks, least-privilege Postgres roles), and release verification. Release archives and `checksums.txt` are now signed keylessly with cosign (GitHub OIDC), each archive has an SPDX SBOM generated by syft, and the release workflow verifies the published signatures in a `verify-signatures` job; `scripts/repro.sh` now checks only the archives, since the release also holds SBOMs and signature bundles.
- `bide view` has a Needs attention list at `/attention` (and `/attention.json`), linked from the run list's headline and its "need a person" count: every run that is waiting for approval (the call, the gate's k of n, who approved and who is pending, how long it has waited), halted with an unknown outcome (with the `agent.ResolveHalt` call), or aborted, newest first, and, from `-db` or `-postgres`, whether the notifier told someone and the sink acknowledged it, plus runs whose recovery keeps failing (read from the notification trail in the same database). The page reloads itself in live mode and works without JavaScript; `internal/runview` has the `Options.Events`, `Options.ApproveCommand` and `Options.Now` fields and `Stores.Events`. The run list is ALL CLEAR only when the notification trail was read for every run and none has a failing recovery; without the trail it says "nothing in the journals needs a person" and that recovery failures are not visible. `bide sample` now seeds a notification trail (`sample.WriteNotifyTrail`) with acknowledged, failing and overdue examples. A page that did not read everything is never clear: a store that cannot list its runs (`CANNOT LIST`), a failed listing or run, runs past the run list's 5,000 cap, or a filter give a headline that says what was not read, and the JSON of both lists carries `status`, `unread`, `truncated` and `listable`. The Needs attention list reads only runs without `run:complete` (no cap), matches a sub-run's notification in its root run's trail, and both lists are cached for two seconds. The list shows the `bide approve` command for each pending approval (quoted, with a fallback for an unsafe id), and `bide view` takes `-postgres-env <VAR>` to read the DSN from the environment. `bide view`'s `-postgres-env` refuses a value that is not an environment variable's name without echoing it.

### Changed

- `bide approve` exits 0 with `already recorded: your approve at <time> counts; nothing to do` when the approver's verified, counted decision is the one they are making again (approve after approve, reject after reject), instead of refusing it as already decided; it signs and writes nothing, points to the existing receipt, and still refuses the opposite verdict, so a retry after a lost response is safe under every signature scheme, including the randomized ecdsa-p256.
- `awskms.Open` now bounds its self-test signature by the context passed to `Open` as well as by `Config.Timeout`, so cancelling that context stops a slow or stalled self-test instead of waiting out the timeout (`Sign` is unchanged). The signer's tests now also run, through the AWS SDK, against the in-process aws-kms-emulator for both Ed25519 and ECDSA P-256, in the new test-only module `signer/awskms/emulatortest` (never tagged, so the emulator is not in the signer's module graph), in addition to the fake client.
- `bide demo` now opens with the same crash without bide, where a naive retry charges the fake provider twice (`provider charges: 2`), and ends on the bide run's single charge. On the halt it tells ops with a signed webhook (the real `agent.WithNotifier` and `notify` webhook) delivered to an in-process HTTPS receiver that verifies the signature, and after the proofs it changes one amount in a copy of the journal and shows the real verifier failing on it. `assets/crash-demo.gif` is re-recorded with the `bide view` page live from the first frame.
- The README and the approval, notifications and audit guides now show real recordings of `bide approve` (a refund at a 2-of-3 gate: the requester refused, then finance reviews the call and its SHA-256 and signs, then risk completes the gate) and of `bide audit notifications` (one run passing on a signed trail, one failing on an approval nobody was notified of), each in dark and light. `assets/demo/approve.sh` and `assets/demo/notifications.sh` regenerate them from `assets/demo/setup/`, which drives the real runtime and notifier, and `assets/demo/optimize-png.py` shrinks screenshots.
- The evidence format is `bide.audit.evidence.v6`: its seal signs the tag and the SHA-256 of the package (60 bytes) instead of the whole JSON, so a KMS with a 4096-byte limit can seal a package of any size. A v5 package still verifies under its original seal (`bide audit verify-evidence` prints a NOTE), `Seal` refuses to re-seal one, and v4 and older are refused with `ErrFormat` as before.
- bide now describes itself as a Go framework for AI agents that move money ("risky calls approved, every action recorded and verifiable"), with a fifth guarantee, people in control, beside the four it already had: the README, its four translations, the docs site home page and description, Concepts, Getting started, the `agent` package documentation and the `bide` help text lead with crash-safe execution, k-of-n signed approvals with separation of duties, a notifier that records its delivery (with routing, escalation, an all-clear and an optionally signed trail), an approval intake and browser page that the application or organization serves, and proof of everything, and say that bide is built toward what a bank asks of such software (keys in an HSM or a cloud KMS, a tamper-evident trail, explicit failure modes) without being certified against or claiming compliance with any standard. No behavior changes; the first line of `bide` with no command now reads "bide: build AI agents that move money, and account for every action."
- `agent.Approve` and `agent.Cancel` take a trailing option (`...ApproveOption`, `...CancelOption`; `Journal.AnswerInterrupt` and `Journal.Signal` take `...AnswerOption`), so they no longer assign to a variable of the old function type without a wrapper; and `Record.ApproverSignature` is no longer non-nil only for a signed m-of-n decision: it also carries a lone `recorded_at` on single approvals, `ResolveHalt` results, end markers, interrupt answers and signals, so test for a signed decision with `Record.Approver() != ""` (or `IsApprovalDecision`), not with `ApproverSignature != nil`.
- `bide notify relay` takes `-state <file>` (`relay.Config.StatePath`), which keeps the delivery IDs it forwarded and the event IDs it saw cleared across restarts, written atomically and bounded by `-dedupe`, so a trigger retried after a restart that comes after its clear no longer reopens the PagerDuty incident; the file is synced with its directory after each rename (Unix) and written at most once per `-state-flush` window (1 s by default, clears at once; a crash can lose the last window of forwarded-delivery entries), duplicate IDs in the file are merged on load, a second relay on the same file is refused by an exclusive operating-system lock (released if the holder crashes), and without `-state` the memory still resets on restart, and a state file that cannot be parsed is refused at start. A refusal from Slack or PagerDuty now logs only an allow-listed error token (`relay.StatusError.Code`, replacing `Body`) instead of the first 200 bytes of the reply.
- `signer/pkcs11`: `Config.RequireNonExtractable` now also refuses a key whose token reports `CKA_NEVER_EXTRACTABLE` or `CKA_ALWAYS_SENSITIVE` as false (`ErrExtractable`), since a key that was extractable once, or not sensitive, may have been copied out before it was marked otherwise; a token that does not report these two attributes is taken at its word on `CKA_EXTRACTABLE` and `CKA_SENSITIVE` as before; `Open` also refuses a PIN shorter than `MinPINLength` (4 characters, SoftHSM2's minimum) with `agent.ErrConfig`, so the PIN scrubbing of error messages cannot garble them. `Config.ProtectedAuthPath` logs in with no PIN on a token with a PIN pad (`CKF_PROTECTED_AUTHENTICATION_PATH`), is refused with `ErrNoProtectedAuthPath` on a token without one, and is exclusive with `Config.PIN`. A key with `CKA_ALWAYS_AUTHENTICATE` is now signed with: the signer logs in as the context-specific user between `C_SignInit` and `C_Sign` with the same PIN source (it failed before), and `Open` skips its self-test signature for such a key.

### Fixed

- Corrected the docs' statement that an approver decision signs the tool call's arguments when stating AWS KMS's 4096-byte Ed25519 limit: it signs the SHA-256 of the arguments plus the run, call, tool and approver names, so a call with large arguments is decided under a KMS Ed25519 key. `bide approve` also no longer builds a default receipt file name over the 255-byte file name limit for very long run, call or approver ids (each part is cut at 56 characters, was 80), and refuses a key file that is a named pipe instead of blocking on it.
- The browser approve page showed the "Note" label of a call with no note, which shifted every label after it one row away from its value (Gate beside "Decided", and so on); the label is now hidden with its value. Found in a real Chrome screenshot; a DOM test covers it.
