- `audit.VerifyApprovals` and `audit.ApprovalEvidence` honor a re-approval tally (`approval-tally:<id>:v3`) only over a tally that predates `agent.DecisionFormat`, as `agent.RecordedTally` does at runtime, so a re-tally injected beside a current-format denial no longer replaces it. `audit.ApprovalEvidence` now includes the superseded tally beside a re-approval tally, and `VerifyApprovals` refuses a re-approval tally without it.
- `signer/awskms`: `Open` pinned whatever key ARN `GetPublicKey` returned, so a redirected endpoint or a wrong answer naming another key, or another account's, was accepted and signed with; it now requires a key ARN or key ID to come back as that key, and an alias ARN to resolve to a key in its own partition, region and account (an alias name to a key ARN), and refuses otherwise with `agent.ErrConfig` before signing. `bide approve` and `bide-hsm` also refuse a key file or PIN file that another user owns, and open it without blocking so a named pipe is refused rather than waited on.
- `agent.SubmitDecision` names a decision's journal step by the decision (call, approver, verdict, scheme and decided_at), never by its signature bytes, so re-signing and resubmitting one decision concurrently with a randomized scheme (ECDSA P-256 from a KMS or HSM) journals one record instead of two, and `agent.ReportWritten` reports exactly one write; `agent.DecisionStep` returns the new name. Journals written under the old signature-derived names read, count and verify unchanged, a resubmission of such a decision writes nothing more, and a record that is not the approver's valid decision never takes the name the real decision needs (it is then journaled under the signature-derived name). An unchecked resubmission of a decision recorded under the old name is still a no-op that reports no write, and `agent.HeldDecisionStep` finds the record that holds a decision wherever it is (with a verifier it also reports whether the record verifies and can count) (`DecisionStep` is the name written first, not a lookup key). The approvals endpoint and `bide approve -to` find a decision's record by what it is rather than by its derived name, so a record under the fallback or an old name is returned as itself; the endpoint answers a request whose bytes equal a record that verifies under no current key and that the gate does not count as an invalid approval rather than a replay, and omits `receipt` from a replay answered from the recorded tally after a key rotation (`Response.Receipt` is now a pointer). `agent.IsDecisionStepName` checks the shape of a decision record name, and `bide approve -to` refuses a record name the endpoint returns that does not have it, and says when the receipt carries a different signature than the one sent.
- `Agent.FlushNotifications` rechecks the events it deferred to a peer under one shared 2 s budget (or the caller's deadline if sooner), not 2 s per event, so a flush with no deadline and a hung trail no longer waits 2 s times the number of deferred events; the events it could not recheck are reported as they are.
- The notifier's holder no longer sends the overdue notice from its own round while a peer's claim on the notice is live, so a slow holder and a peer no longer both send it; the holder sends it in a later round if the peer's send never lands.
- `bide audit notifications` no longer lets a verdict rest on records nothing attests. An event whose observation, acknowledgement or clearing is only among the records after the newest signed tree head is now `unattested` (a problem) and fails the audit, because anyone who can append to the stored trail could have written it; the report gives the tail's size and time range, and a nonzero `-max-unattested` (library `MaxTailAge`) accepts it, without a signature, when its tail records are that recent; a tail-only `failing_since` or `recovered` record, which has no event key, is judged the same way as an `unattested` recovery-state verdict. New `-routing` (library `Routing`; write it with the new `agent.RoutingOf` from the notifier's own options, whose `NotifyRouting.Receivers` the notifier now routes by) requires each event a delivery to the receivers it routes to, and each due escalation step a delivery to its receiver, else `no_delivery`; without it per-receiver completeness is not checked, which is documented. The verifier also reads the notifier's own fan-out and escalation trails: a delivery to several receivers (`to:<receiver>:<key>`, the default receiver recorded as no name) and an escalation step (which carries its event's operation) no longer fail the operation and receiver checks, and an end-to-end test runs the real engine under every routing shape and verifies the trail, signed and unsigned.
- `WithOutputMode` on an untyped run is now `ErrConfig` at the run's first drive, before anything is journaled or any model call is made, as the documentation says; only a later drive of the run refused it before.
- The Release workflow used `sigstore/cosign-installer@v4`, a tag that does not exist (the action publishes only full versions such as `v4.1.2`), so the first tag pushed since release signing was added would have failed before building anything; it now pins `v4.1.2`. A `workflow_dispatch` dry run of the workflow showed it.
- `bide view` and `bide approve` show a gate whose recorded tally passed without excluding the requester, under a policy that now excludes it, as waiting for approval again (`agent.PendingSoDReapproval`), with the requester's decision not counted, instead of as decided.
- `govern/sqlitelog` and `store/sqlite` no longer fail with `SQLITE_BUSY` ("database is locked") when many writers in one process append to one file at once, which a slow disk (the Windows CI runner) made the shared conformance test hit after its 30 second wait: SQLite's busy handler is not a queue, so a writer that had just released the lock won it back and one unlucky writer waited out everyone else's whole run; the writers in a process now take turns on each file in the order they ask, and `govern/sqlitelog` sets its busy timeout in the connection string so that every connection the pool opens has it (it was set on the first connection only). Writers in different processes are still not ordered, which `docs/KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md` states. Lease statements (`store/sqlite` acquire, renew and release) go ahead of the queued journal writes, so a renewal is not starved by a burst of writers; files are matched by identity (device and inode, volume and file index), so every spelling of a path shares one queue. `store/sqlite` and `govern/sqlitelog` now open the canonical path (symlinks resolved), and refuse a file with more than one hard link with an `ErrConfig` error, because two handles reaching one WAL-mode file by different names make SQLite derive different -wal and -shm names and corrupt it (seen on Windows as "database disk image is malformed" through a symlink, and as a disk I/O error through a hard link on macOS).

## [0.16.0] - 2026-10-13

### Added

- A JSON Schema (Draft 2020-12) for flow configs, at `plan/schema/flow.schema.json` (`$id` `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/bide-ai/bide/main/plan/schema/flow.schema.json`), so editors complete and check a config as it is typed: keys with hover descriptions, the `safety` values, the edge/switch/join forms of a wiring element, the approval block, and a pattern for each form of the `assert` grammar. Point an editor at it with a `# yaml-language-server: $schema=<url>` first line (now on every config in `examples/plan/flows`), a VS Code `yaml.schemas` mapping or a JetBrains JSON Schema mapping (see the "Editor support" section of the plan flows guide). `plan/schema_test.go` keeps it the same as the code: every key `plan.Load` decodes is in the schema and no other, its enums and assertion patterns are the code's own, a table of good and bad configs is accepted and refused alike by the schema and by `Load`, and every sample config is valid under it. The schema checks the file's shape only; names, types and whether an assertion holds are still decided by `Load` and `bide check`.
- A run made of `Journal.Step` calls alone has no `run:start`; `bide view` listed it as "not started" even when it had halted and been resolved. It is now labeled "steps only (no run:start)" in the run list and on the run page, with a state read from the journal: halted (a live attempt marker with no result), resolved (`agent.ResolveHalt` recorded an outcome) or steps done. A journal holding only its header is still not started. The run list's kind for such a run is `steps` in `/runs.json` and `/run.json`.
- `agent.NotifyPending(ctx, j, notifier, trail, opts...)`: one catch-up sweep for the notifier, for an application that drives runs with `Run` alone and so has no `RecoverLoop` to find an event whose process died before it was recorded. It reads every unfinished run and delivers, as `WithNotifier` would and under the same event keys (so an event a drive also saw is delivered once), each run waiting at an approval gate and each run halted on an effect whose outcome is unknown. A halt is a marker with no result and no voiding not-started record, older than `WithNotifyHaltAge` (default 5 minutes), because a sweep cannot see a process that is still running the call. It returns the deliveries that failed, which the next sweep retries ([design](docs/design/notifier.md)).
- `bide doctor`: checks what a first run depends on and prints a result with a fix hint for each: the Go version (1.27 or later), `bide` against the latest release (skipped offline), that SQLite works in a temporary directory, a Postgres DSN from `BIDE_POSTGRES_DSN` (or the variable `-postgres-env` names) if set, which model API key variables are set (names only, never values), that `GOPATH` and the module cache are writable, and in a `bide init` project that it builds. Colored on a terminal like the other commands (`-color`, `NO_COLOR`). Exit 1 only when a check failed.
- `bide sample` writes a sample journal and opens `bide view` on it, with no code, no API key and no network: 14 runs produced by the real runtime on a scripted model and fake tools (refund agent runs completed, halted with an unknown outcome, halted and resolved, waiting at a 2-of-3 approval gate, approved with signatures, in progress, aborted and cancelled; plan flow runs completed, waiting, approved and halted; a run of durable steps only), two evidence packages with the log and approver keys that verify them (`bide audit verify-evidence`, `bide view -evidence -approver-keys`), three flow configs (a v1, a v2 that weakens the approval gate, and one with a violated assertion, for `bide view -config`, `bide check` and `bide check -diff -fail-on-weaken`), and a `README.txt` of what is inside and the commands to try. `bide sample -out DIR -no-browser` prints the viewer's address instead of opening a browser, and `-live` keeps one more run progressing while you watch. The runs are the same on every call (run IDs, states, start times and records), except for the random salt each record carries, so digests differ; the keys are fixed sample keys, not for anything real. The run viewer guide's run list, halted flow, risk map and violated-assertion screenshots are taken of it (`assets/demo/shots.sh`).
- `bide view` shows the proof and the approvals, and gets its finishing touches. An approval gate's approvers are cards: initials, what they decided, whether their signature verified (and why not), the fingerprint of their key (with `-approver-keys`), and when they decided (signed) and when bide recorded it. With a signed head or an evidence package the run page draws a proof path: the last covered record's leaf hash, the sibling hash at each level of its RFC 6962 audit path and the signed root, built from the proof the audit package verified (the page re-climbs the path for the intermediate hashes and draws nothing if it does not end at the root). A light and dark toggle (remembered in local storage, guarded for blocked storage), keyboard shortcuts (`j` and `k` through the records or runs, `/` to filter, `t`, `s`, `?` for help), a filter box on the timeline, a tab icon colored by the verdict and a title per page (`VERIFIED · refund-42 · bide view`), empty states for a list or filter with nothing, and a loading bar while a live page fetches. **Share this run** saves a 1200 by 630 PNG of the verdict, key numbers, graph or proof path, and proof line, drawn in the browser from the page with nothing sent anywhere. The pages still ask for no other host and carry no inline script or style.
- A durable `audit.EventStore` in `store/sqlite` and `store/postgres`: `(*Store).EventStore()` keeps a run's event trail in the same database as its journal, in a `bide_events` table (created on `Open`/`New`; the table prefix and schema apply; the schema version is unchanged and an older database gains the table). `Append` is one insert-if-absent statement on primary key `(run_id, seq)`, committed before it returns, so it is crash-safe and writers racing for a position across connections or nodes have exactly one winner. New `audit.ErrSeqTaken`, wrapped by `EventStore.Append` when a different leaf already holds the position, lets a caller tell a lost race or a fork from a real failure (`MemEventStore` returns it too); a negative `seq` is now refused. New `audit/eventstoretest`, a conformance suite for any `EventStore` (append and load, byte fidelity, the same-leaf no-op, `ErrSeqTaken`, gaps, racing writers), run against the in-memory store and both new ones ([#239]).
- `examples/stripe-refund`: a refund agent against Stripe's TEST mode API, killed mid-refund. It looks up a payment intent (read-only), gates a refund over a threshold on 2-of-3 approval, sends `POST /v1/refunds` with an `Idempotency-Key` and metadata naming the run and call, and writes a receipt, on a SQLite journal. `-crash-after-send` exits the process right after the request is sent and before its result is journaled; the restart halts with `OutcomeUnknown` instead of refunding again, and a reconciler lists the payment intent's refunds, matches the step's key in their metadata, and resolves the halt with `ResolveHalt` (`WithMinHaltAge`, the refund as evidence), so exactly one refund exists. It resolves nothing when Stripe has no such refund. The example calls Stripe with `net/http` (no SDK, no new dependency), reads `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` from the environment and refuses anything but an `sk_test_` key. Its tests run the binary as separate processes against a fake Stripe that honors idempotency keys and need no key; `demo.sh` and the README do the kill and restart against the real test API by hand. A new example module (`examples/stripe-refund`), listed in CI, `go.work` and the release script's repo-only modules.
- Hardening from an independent review: the overdue notice is derived from the trail and sent inside the round that holds the event's claim (so it survives a restart and a failed first delivery); every `Notify` call is bounded by a quarter of the claim and rounds of different events run concurrently, so one hung sink does not stall the rest, and `RecoverLoop` returns on cancel; another holder's claim counts as live for a skew margin past its `until`, and a new round is paced by the trail, so the claims of an undelivered event stay near four an hour whatever the number of sweeps; a decided gate (single decision, tally, tool or node result) is cleared, not reported; `NotifyPending` also finds aborted runs; any pause closes a recovery failure episode, a run no `Resumer` drives alerts, and a shutdown's cancellation does not; `recovery_failing` carries a class, never the Resumer's error text; an idle notifier holds no goroutine; `FlushNotifications` returns what it could not deliver; a plan flow keeps one notifier per store. A peer sends the overdue notice, under a short claim of its own, when the holder that was to send it may be gone, and a round's budget counts its backoff and acknowledgement retries so it ends inside its claim.
- `plan.Builder.WithNotifier(notifier, trail, opts...)` and `plan.WithLoadedNotifier` tell an `agent.Notifier`, at least once and with a stable ID, when a run that `Flow.Run` drives halts on an unknown outcome, waits at an approval gate, or is aborted, as `agent.WithNotifier` does for an agent run. The binding is runtime only (the digest is unchanged), is validated by `Build`, and keeps one notifier per journal the flow runs against; `Flow.FlushNotifications` waits for deliveries in flight. `agent.NewRunNotifier` and `RunNotifier.DriveEnd` let code that drives its own runs do the same.
- Recipe: [get notified when a run needs a human](docs/recipes/notify-human.md). A complete program that posts one JSON message to a webhook (a Slack incoming webhook, say) the first time a run halts on an unknown outcome or waits for approval, from the three places bide reports it (the error `Run` returns, the `Resumer` that `RecoverLoop` calls, and a `MemWaker` wakeup), and not again on every recovery pass. It says plainly what its in-process dedupe cannot do (a restart may notify again, a crash before the POST may drop the message) and points to the built-in notifier, which closes those gaps. Linked from the recipes index, the docs site sidebar, the README and its translations, and getting started.
- Recipes: five complete flows in [`docs/recipes`](docs/recipes/index.md), each runnable with no API key and with a real model, with what `bide view` shows and what a crash does. A refund with a 2-of-3 approval over a threshold (from `bide init`), invoice approval (a model extracts, a person approves, then the invoice is booked), support ticket triage (a read-only model call routes to side-effect nodes), updating a customer record safely (the attempt marker, the halt on an unknown outcome, `agent.ResolveHalt`, and an idempotent write), and an ambient agent (a durable `agent.Sleep` that survives a restart). Linked from the docs site nav and sidebar, the docs index, the README and its four translations, and getting started. CI compiles every Go block (`docsnip`).
- The `bide` command colors its text output on a terminal, for a reader who scans it. `bide check` marks each error with a red ✗, splits a violated assertion over the assertion, its reason and its counterexample path (in red, with the node it reaches in bold), so each line fits the terminal, colors risk classes as the risk map does (side effect red, read-only and idempotent green, an approval gate amber) and the verdict counts, and marks a config whose assertions all hold with a green ✓; `bide check -diff -check` colors added lines green, removed red and changed amber, and calls out a weakened approval gate first, in bold red, with the summary's counts and digest on lines of their own. `bide audit` marks `PASS`/`OK` lines with a green ✓ and `FAIL` lines with a red ✗, and a verify verb ends with a `VERIFIED` (green, with the signed tree's size and Merkle root dimmed) or `FAILED` (red) banner; the note `verify-evidence` puts on every approver decision line is said once, before the banner. `bide demo` gives each step its color (the crash red, the halt amber, the resolution blue, the proofs green) and now names the halt and the resolution in words ("halted: outcome unknown", "resolved with ResolveHalt"); `bide view` and `bide init` print the address to open and the next commands in bold cyan. Status is never carried by color alone: the words stay, and a mark is added beside them. Written to a file or a pipe, and under `bide audit -json`, the output is the plain text it was (golden tests pin it byte for byte). `init`, `demo`, `view`, `check` and `audit` take `-color=auto|always|never` (default `auto`); `auto` colors a terminal only, not with `NO_COLOR` set to any value or `TERM=dumb`, and on Windows only a terminal known to read ANSI escapes (Windows Terminal, ConEmu, ANSICON, VS Code, or a `TERM` such as `xterm-256color`). The styles are package `internal/term`, standard library only, so `bide audit` and the `bide_audit_only` build stay core-only. Help (`-h`) is unchanged. Screenshots in the [run viewer](docs/guides/run-viewer.md#color) and [audit](docs/guides/audit.md#cli-reference-bide-audit) guides, recorded by `assets/demo/cli.sh`; `assets/crash-demo.gif` is re-recorded in color.
- The notification trail, the first building block of the built-in notifier ([design](docs/design/notifier.md)): `agent.NotifyRecord` and its kinds, the key builders (`HaltedKey`, `ApprovalKey`, `AbortedKey`, `RecoveryKey`, `NotifyID`), `agent.FoldNotify` (a pure fold of a trail to the state of each event and of the run's recovery failure episode), the `agent.NotifyTrail` port with `agent.MemNotifyTrail`, and `audit.NewNotifyTrail`, which stores a trail as salted, Merkle-committed event leaves on any `audit.EventStore` and turns a lost append race into `agent.ErrTrailConflict`. `agenttest.NotifyTrailContract` is the contract test for a trail. Nothing sends a notification yet.
- The notifier core: `agent.WithNotifier(notifier, trail)` tells a `Notifier`, at least once and with a stable `Notification.ID`, when a run halts on an unknown outcome, waits for approval, or is aborted (a saga whose rollback finished), and when recovery keeps failing for a run. It applies to an `Agent` (the event is raised where a drive ends), and to `Recover` and `RecoverLoop` (which also raise `recovery_failing`, counted in time from a `failing_since` record in the trail so a crash loop that restarts the process still alerts). Delivery state lives in a `NotifyTrail`, never in the run's journal: observed, claimed, acknowledged, failing, overdue and cleared records, so two processes cannot both deliver while a claim is live, a restart does not repeat an acknowledged event, a halt resolved or a gate decided before delivery is cleared instead of sent, and an event not acknowledged by its deadline (`WithNotifyDeadline`, default 5 minutes) raises an overdue notice to `WithOverdueNotifier` and keeps retrying. Retries never give up (capped backoff, `WithNotifyRetry`), and `WithNotifyErrors` hears when a delivery is failing. `Agent.FlushNotifications` waits for deliveries in flight. A Notifier reports how a delivery was authenticated with `agent.ReportNotifyAck`. The webhook notifier, the `NotifyPending` sweep and `bide audit` verification follow ([design](docs/design/notifier.md)).
- The `notify` package ships bide's webhook notifier, `notify.NewWebhook`, an `agent.Notifier` that delivers a notification as a signed HTTPS POST of a `bide.notification.v1` JSON payload. The payload holds structured fields only (kind, run, step and tool name, the approval gate's approver IDs and k of n, the run's principal, times and a `bide view` pointer): no error text, tool arguments or approve link, unless the operator names fields in `WithFields(notify.ErrorText, notify.Args("refund"))`. Each request carries `Bide-Delivery` (the stable notification ID), `Bide-Timestamp` and `Bide-Signature: v1=<hex HMAC-SHA256(timestamp.body)>`, one entry per secret so a secret can be rotated with `WithSecrets(current, previous)`. `notify.Verify(r, secrets, tolerance)` checks a request in one call for a receiver (5 minute default timestamp tolerance, constant-time compare, one MAC per secret and at most 8 signature entries examined, the `Bide-Delivery` header required to equal the signed body's `id`, body left readable). Transport is HTTPS only (`http` only for localhost, 127.0.0.1 and [::1]), never follows redirects, times out per request, makes one request per `Notify` call (the notifier core runs the rounds; `WithRequestsPerCall` allows up to 3 with backoff), honors `Retry-After` on a 429 or 5xx within the call's deadline, and requires TLS 1.2 or newer. Optional mutual TLS (`WithClientCert`, reloaded when the files change without a restart, keeping the last good pair if a reload fails), `WithServerCA` (replaces the system roots) and `WithServerPin` (SPKI SHA-256, up to two) add to the signature and never replace it. Each accepted delivery reports how it was authenticated (signature scheme and secret slot, never a secret; the client certificate's SPKI hash; custom CA or pin; negotiated TLS version) for the `auth` field of the trail's `acked` record. See the new [notifications guide](docs/guides/notifications.md) for Slack and PagerDuty adapters.
- TLA+ model 14 (`spec/tla/notify`) of the notifier's notify-once trail (observed, one claim per delivery round, acked, failing, overdue, cleared, failing_since, recovered) and its overdue notice, sent by the event's holder inside its round or by a peer under a short claim on the notice's key, over processes, crashes, lost replies, trail appends that error, claims that lapse mid-send and the lost append race. It checks no silent drop, a duplicate bound of 1 + crashes + lost replies + mid-round lapses (2 + the same for the notice), no concurrent double under a live claim, overdue by the deadline and its notice sent even when the holder is dead, `recovery_failing` under a crash loop, idempotent `acked` and `cleared`, and, composed with model 10, that the notifier never changes the run lifecycle. A regression configuration per property breaks its rule and must fail. It found N1, adopted by the core: a failure count kept in process memory is reset by a restart, so `failing` came late and, under a crash loop, never. `spec/tla/check.sh` gains `SPEC`, `LIBRARY` and `EXPECT: action` configuration lines, and a pull request that changes model 10 re-checks model 14.

### Changed

- A second color pass over the CLI, on a terminal only (piped output is byte for byte what it was). Every error `bide` prints to stderr opens with a bold red `error:` (a hint after a semicolon is dim, in `bide init`'s messages), `bide` and `bide help` show bold headings and command names over dim summaries, and `bide version` is lightly styled. `bide version` takes `-color` like the other commands. The project `bide init` writes gets a `color.go` that colors its story (paused at the gate, approvals, done) with the same rules (a terminal, not `NO_COLOR`, not `TERM=dumb`), with a test of them; `examples/approval` and `examples/stripe-refund` color their narration the same way.
- **Breaking:** every signed m-of-n approval decision now carries two times, kept apart. `decided_at` is when the approver decided, by their clock, and is inside the signature: `ApprovalDecisionBytes` takes it (`ApprovalDecisionBytes(subject, approverID, approved, decidedAt)`), `Decision.DecidedAt` is required (`SubmitDecision` refuses a zero one with `ErrConfig`), and the signed encoding is now `bide.approval.v3` (`agent.DecisionFormat`), which appends `len32(decided_at) decided_at` after the decision byte (the m-of-n design note works one decision through to its exact bytes, pinned by `TestApprovalDecisionBytes_Vector`). It is signed in one form only, RFC 3339 in UTC to the millisecond with a `Z`, years 0001 to 9999 (`agent.FormatTimestamp`, 24 bytes, the form of `started_at`); a verifier parses it, formats it again and refuses the decision unless the two are byte for byte the same (`agent.ParseTimestamp`, `agent.ReasonDecidedAt`), so a decision whose time was changed after signing does not verify. **Decisions signed under the previous encoding (`bide.approval.v2`) no longer verify**: the gate and `audit.VerifyApprovals` ignore them with `agent.ReasonNoDecidedAt`, there is no fallback, and they must be signed again. **A run whose gate passed on v2 decisions before the upgrade, and whose call has not run, does not run it on resume**: the gate asks again (`*ApprovalPending` with `Reason` `agent.PendingReapproval`, its `Quorum.Supersedes` naming the old tally), and once approvers sign under v3 it records the new outcome under `agent.ApprovalRetallyStep`, which the gate, `audit.ApprovalEvidence`, `audit.VerifyApprovals` and `bide view` read in place of the old tally (`agent.RecordedTally`); a v2 denial stays final. Every journaled tally now names its format (`ApprovalTally.DecisionFormat`). `recorded_at` (`Record.RecordedAt`) is when `SubmitDecision` journaled the decision, by the submitting process's clock (`WithClock`, now also an `ApproveOption`, else the run clock on the context, else the wall clock; one with no canonical form is `ErrConfig`): that submitter's claim, not signed, anchored by the journal's Merkle trail once a signed head covers it. `ApprovalWait` (`approval-pending`) records when the run paused, and a plan flow gate's `ApprovalRequest` when it recorded the request: when approvers were asked. Both times are display only: `TallyApprovals` checks `decided_at` only as signed bytes and orders decisions by journal position, so the same decisions count the same whatever their times (`TestTally_IgnoresTimes`), and a decision signed a day ahead still counts (`TestApprovalTimes_FlagsNeverUncount`). A decision's journal name now hashes `decided_at` too, so a copy of a valid signature journaled first under another time cannot take the name the real decision needs, and an approver whose identical decision was recorded before a flow gate's request signs again at a new time and counts (the seat is no longer lost; `Flows.tla`'s `ADecide` and the new `flows/flow-gate-early` configuration, which replaces the `gate-early-stuck` limit); `WithDecisionCheck` reads, as the gate does, only decisions after a flow gate's request. `audit.ApprovalEvidence` includes the gate's `approval-pending` record (a `step` item) when the journal holds one, and `audit.VerifyApprovals` reports each counted decision's two times (`ApprovalVerdict.Times`, `AskedAt`, `HeadAt`) with sanity flags that never change the verdict (`audit.CheckDecisionTimes`, tolerance `DefaultDecisionTimeTolerance`, 5 minutes): `FlagDecidedAfterRecorded`, `FlagDecidedBeforeAsked`, `FlagAskedUnknown`, `FlagRecordedAtInvalid`, `FlagRecordedOutOfOrder` and `FlagRecordedAfterHead`. `bide audit verify-approvals` prints both times, the gap and a `note:` per flag, with `-decision-time-tolerance`, and prints any time that is not canonical quoted, so an unsigned field cannot add lines to its report; `bide view`'s approver cards show the times in place of the signed head's time, and only the counted decision's when an approver has several. `SubmitDecision` refuses an `ApproverID` that is not printable ASCII (no policy can name one), both `bide audit` verbs print any text from an input file that holds a control character quoted, and a plan flow run with a re-approval tally conforms (`Flow.Conform`). `ApproveOption` is now an interface (`WithDecisionCheck` and `WithClock` implement it). An RFC 3161 trusted timestamp per decision is possible future work.
- `bide audit` errors a first verification meets say what to do next: a missing evidence file, a `-pubkey` that is not a key (it takes `ed25519:<hex>`, 64 hex digits or a file such as `keys/log.pub`), a file that is not the JSON a flow or `bide audit` writes (such as the journal database), and an evidence package that did not verify (check the key came from out of band and the file is unmodified). Exit statuses are unchanged.
- Errors a newcomer is most likely to hit now say what to do next, with their sentinels (`errors.Is`) unchanged. A halted run (`*OutcomeUnknown`) names `agent.ResolveHalt` and re-running the run; a run waiting for approval (`*ApprovalPending`) names `SubmitDecision`; resuming a run with another input, flow, kind or saga mode suggests the original or a new run ID; `agent.New` with a nil model or journal names what to pass; a `plan.Load` config that names an unknown block says to register it and lists what is registered, and an unused block or duplicate node says how to fix it; a flow run recorded under another flow or topology digest says the flow changed and to resume with the original or start a new run ID; a provider's 401 or 403 says to check the API key and a 404 the model name and base URL; `sqlite.Open`, `sqlite.OpenReadOnly` and `postgres.Open` failures say what to check (a missing directory or file, a file that is not a journal, the DSN). Message text only: no behavior changes.
- `run:aborted` records when a saga's finished rollback ended the run: its value is now an object with the reason (`reason`, as before) and `aborted_at` (UTC, RFC 3339 with milliseconds, by the drive's clock), where it was the reason as a JSON string. The time is informational, like `started_at`: no drive, resume or recovery pass reads it, `agent.Status` reads the reason from either form, and a `run:aborted` an earlier version wrote loads and behaves the same. The notifier's catch-up sweep uses it to bound which aborted runs it raises.
- The `bide view` pages are redesigned. Every page opens with a verdict in large type, with a glyph and a line of why, and three or four key numbers: a run is `VERIFIED`, `COMPLETED`, `HALTED`, `WAITING FOR APPROVAL`, `FAILED` or `IN PROGRESS`; a config is `N POLICY VIOLATIONS` or `ALL ASSERTIONS HOLD` (or errors, undecided assertions, none); a diff opens with `APPROVAL GATE WEAKENED` when a gate was weakened; the run list opens with what needs a person. The cards under it are condensed to a label, a value and a line, with the detail in `details`. The flow graph (run path overlay, risk map, diff) is a hand-drawn layered SVG from an in-house layout, replacing the earlier grid: rounded nodes with a glyph per risk class or state, a `2/3` gate badge, edges routed around nodes, and the run path or counterexample as a flowing dash (still under `prefers-reduced-motion`). Hovering a node highlights the paths through it, clicking shows its details, and the graph pans and zooms; without JavaScript it stays a complete static SVG. The timeline has a glyph per record type, an anchor (`#record-N`) on every row, a collapsible payload with highlighted JSON and a copy button, and, for the records the journal timestamps (the start and attempt markers), an offset from the start with the UTC time on hover and a span bar. One set of design tokens (color, space, type scale) serves both themes, which keep following `prefers-color-scheme`. The JSON views (`/runs.json`, `/run.json`, `/config.json`, `/diff.json`) are unchanged, and the pages make no request to another host.

### Fixed

- A notification's `view.command` was `bide view -run <id>`, which fails because `bide view` needs a store. The webhook notifier now takes `notify.WithViewDB(path)` and the command is `bide view -db <path> -run <id>`; without it the command is the marked template `bide view -db <journal.db> -run <id>` rather than one that fails when pasted. The notifications guide and its Slack adapter say so.
- An audit verb run without a required flag, such as `bide audit verify-approvals` without `-call`, `-need` or `-approvers`, printed about 95 lines of usage for every verb and no line saying what was missing. It now prints one line that names the missing flags and says to run `bide audit <verb> -h`, and exits 2 as before. Every audit verb does the same.
- `bide demo -fast`, documented as for CI, served the viewer until Ctrl-C. It now exits 0 when the demo is done, without serving or opening a browser; with `-keep` it prints the `bide view` command that reopens the run.
- `bide doctor` failed on a Go older than 1.27 even where `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` (the default) lets go fetch 1.27 itself inside a bide project. It is now a warning there, and still a failure under `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` or a pinned older toolchain. The fix text names `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.27.0` as the getting-started guide does, long lines wrap so the output fits 120 columns, and in color the check names line up whether a row has a mark or not.
- `MemWaker.Start` no longer runs a `Fire` after its context is cancelled. A select picks at random among ready cases, so a ready tick could win over the done context, and the loop would resume due runs with a cancelled context during shutdown (still before the stopped channel closed); it now checks the context again after each tick (`TestMemWaker_StartNothingFiresAfterCancel`).
- `RecoverLoop` no longer starts a drive or a recovery pass after its context is cancelled. A select picks at random among ready cases, so a free drive slot or a pass tick could win over the done context, and the loop could lease a run and call the `Resumer` with a cancelled context during shutdown (still before `RecoverLoop` returned); it now checks the context again after each wait (`TestRecoverLoop_NothingStartsAfterCancel`).
- The run viewer's proof-path diagram clipped the signed-time label ("UTC" read "UT"). The SVG is wider, and the label sits on the side of the root with no sibling hash.

## [0.15.0] - 2026-10-13

### Added

- `bide view` with no `-run` opens on a run list: each run's ID (linking to its page), kind, state (completed, halted: outcome unknown, waiting for approval, in progress, aborted, cancelled; the state the run page shows), when it started (`RunStart.StartedAt`; "none" for a run journaled by an earlier version, which recorded no start time) and its record count, newest first. It filters by state (with counts), kind and run ID substring, shows 50 rows with a "show more" link, and serves the same list as JSON at `/runs.json`. It reads SQLite, Postgres and exported journals, read-only, on loopback only, with no JavaScript. One load classifies at most 5,000 runs and says when it stops short. The guide's screenshot is of a demo journal any checkout can write (`BIDE_RUNLIST_DEMO=$PWD/runs.db go test ./cmd/bide -run TestRunListDemo`).
- A run's `run:start` records when its first drive started: `RunStart.StartedAt` (`started_at`), UTC, RFC 3339 with milliseconds, by the drive's clock (`WithClock`, else the wall clock), for agent runs, session turns and plan flow runs; `RunStart.Started` reads it as a `time.Time`. It is informational, for tools that list runs: no drive, resume, recovery pass or at-most-once check reads it, and a later drive neither rewrites it nor is held to it. A run whose `run:start` was written by an earlier version has none and loads and resumes as before (`TestRunStart_StartedAtIsNotRead` drives the same journal with the time as written, absent, different and not a time, and checks every drive does and journals the same; `TestRunStart_EarlierJournalLoads`). A replayed run (`Replay`) records its own start time, so its journal matches the original's but for that member. The recovery pass's fast decoding of the commonest `run:start` reads the new member too. Journal format: one new optional member, with no format version change before 1.0.
- Risk map and policy assertions for flow configs. `bide view -config` colors each node by its crash behavior (side effect: the resume halts and asks; read-only or idempotent: it re-runs; unknown without the Registry), marks its approval gate, and sums up side effects behind a gate and not ("1 side-effect step, 1 gated, 0 ungated"). A config's new `assert` list, which `plan.Load` ignores, states policy about the graph: `every path to <node> passes approval >= K [of N]`, `every path to <node> passes <node>`, `every path to a terminal passes <node>`, `no side effect after <node>`, `<node> reachable` and `<node> unreachable`, each decided by reachability over the declared graph (loops included). A violated assertion is an error with a shortest counterexample path, drawn in red on the page and printed by `bide check`, which exits 1; an ill-formed, unknown-node or undecided assertion is an error too. `plan.InspectConfig` reports them in `ConfigReport.Safety`, `.Gated` and `.Assertions` (`plan.NodeSafety`, `plan.AssertionResult`). `examples/plan/flows/refund-policy.yaml` and `refund-policy-bypass.yaml` show a holding and a violated policy.
- `bide view` live mode: the page of a run in progress, read from a SQLite or Postgres journal, follows the run. Its timeline rows appear as records land, and END STATE, the approval gates and the flow graph update in place (scroll position kept), with a "live" mark, until the run journals its end marker. The page's one embedded script polls the new `/run/live?id=<run>&after=<cursor>` about once a second; the viewer answers from one `Load` of the entries after the cursor (the store sequence of the last record shown) and returns only the new records' rows. Read-only and loopback-only like the rest of the viewer; an exported journal stays static, and the page is complete without JavaScript. `examples/approval run -turn-delay` holds each model turn so it can be watched; `assets/demo/live.sh` records the guide's GIF.
- `bide check flow.yaml -diff <old>` compares a flow config with an earlier version: a read-only page draws the new flow with added nodes and edges in green, removed ones as dashed red ghosts and changed nodes in amber (block, `safety`, approval gate: `approval 1 of 3 -> 2 of 3`), with a summary card (counts, the digest before and after) and a weakened approval policy called out first. `<old>` is a file or, when no file has that name, a git revision (`<rev>:<path>`, or a bare `<rev>` for the same path). `-check` prints one line per change and a summary, for CI, and exits 0 unless `-fail-on-weaken` finds a gate removed or weakened (a set of approvals the new gate accepts would not have passed the old one). `bide view -config <file> -diff <old>` is the same page, and package `plan/planview` serves it (`DiffHandler`, `CheckDiff`) with each version's `plan.Registry`, for the digests. `bide check` now accepts flags after the file names. Samples: `examples/plan/flows/refund-v1.yaml` and `refund-v2.yaml`.

## [0.14.1] - 2026-10-13

### Changed

- A plan flow `Model` node (`Builder.Model`, `Registry.RegisterModel`) is `ReadOnly` by default, so a model call whose outcome is unknown (a crash lost its result, or the call failed: the provider errored or the reply did not decode) is made again on the next drive instead of halting with "unknown outcome and is not retry-safe". A model node calls no tool and changes nothing outside the run; a re-call costs tokens and may answer differently, and nothing saw the lost answer. A config `safety: side_effect` keeps the old halt. A node a v0.14 process already attempted keeps its attempt marker and still halts; resolve it with `ResolveHalt` or start a new run. Found by a fresh-eyes run of the `bide init` project on a real model.

### Fixed

- A plan flow `Model` node now sends its output type's JSON schema as the request's response format to a model that honours one (`agent.ModelInfoOf` reports `ResponseFormat`: `model/openai`, `model/gemini`), as `Builder.Model` documented, so the provider's structured output returns the type's JSON. It sent none, and a real model (gpt-4o-mini) wrapped its JSON in a code fence and put a number in a string field, failing the `bide init` project's model step. A model that does not honour a response format (`model/anthropic`, or one that does not describe itself) is still sent none.
- The `bide init` project: its prompt names each field's type; its README has a "Start over" command that runs in zsh (it named globs that zsh refuses when nothing matches) and removes `evidence.json` too, and says to start over after switching to a real model (a finished run is read back, so the new model was never called); its test runs the story on `model/openai` against a fake provider that answers as a real model did (a fenced reply, a number in a string field) unless sent the schema, and runs the reset command in sh and zsh. The root README says `bide view` prints the address to open (it opens no browser and takes no `-no-browser` flag). Found by a fresh-eyes run on a real model.
- Documentation: every guide, reference page, translation and example README was checked against the current code and corrected where it had drifted; `docs-site/` no longer tracks its synced copies of `tour.md` and `formal-verification.md` (the site build copies them from `docs/`).

## [0.14.0] - 2026-10-13

### Fixed

- `eval.Run` could start executions after its context was cancelled: the launcher's select between a free concurrency slot and the done context picked at random when both were ready. It now checks the context again after taking a slot. Found as a CI flake in `TestRun_CancelledEvalIsAnError`; `TestRun_NoExecutionStartsAfterCancel` reproduces it.

### Added

- The `bide init` project recovers from a crash: it starts with an `agent.Recover` pass whose Resumer drives the plan flow again from its journaled input, so a run an earlier process left unfinished completes, with its refund still made once. `BIDE_CRASH=notify go run .` kills the process mid-run to try it; the project's test does the same in a child process and checks one refund. `model()` has a marked block that swaps in a real model (`model/openai`, for OpenRouter or OpenAI) when `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` or `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set; the scripted model stays the default.
- `bide init <dir>` scaffolds a project that runs with no API key: a plan flow (`flow.yaml`, checked by `bide check`) with a model node on a scripted model (and the lines that swap in a real one), a refund node behind a 2-of-3 approval gate, a SQLite journal, and a `go run .` that pauses at the gate, records two signed approvals with keys it generates, refunds once and writes the evidence `bide view` and `bide audit verify-approvals` read. The project requires bide at the command's own release version (`-dev <checkout>` for a build from source). Its own test runs in CI against the checkout, and `scripts/release.sh` runs the released `bide init` and builds and tests the project against the released modules.
- Approval gates on plan flow nodes. A node gated with `plan.Approval(policy)`, by the agent tool it wraps, or by a config `approval: {need, approvers}` block (on a node or a `join`) pauses before it runs with `*agent.ApprovalPending`, whose `ToolUseID` is the node's key (`node:<name>`, or `node:iter:<n>:<name>` in a loop body, where each iteration is approved on its own) and whose `Args` are the node's input; approvers decide with `agent.SubmitDecision` or `agent.Approve`, and the next `Run` counts them with the agent's own gate (the same records, `TallyApprovals`, the terminal tally before the node runs, `approval-pending:`). A denied gate stops the run with `plan.ErrApprovalDenied`. `Builder.WithApproverVerifiers` and `plan.WithLoadedApproverVerifiers` bind the approver keys; `Build` refuses an m-of-n gate without them and an option or config block that would replace a tool's own gate. The gate records its request under `approval-request:<node key>` (`agent.ApprovalRequest`, `agent.ApprovalRequestStep`), which `agent.FindToolCall` returns for a plan node key; `approval-request:` is a reserved step-name prefix. Only decisions recorded after that request count, and in a flow's run `agent.Approve` and `agent.SubmitDecision` refuse (`ErrConfig`) a decision on a node key with no request yet, since node keys can be known before a run reaches the gate; an identical decision whose name a record written before the request already holds is reported (`ErrConfig`) rather than silently dropped. `Topology` reports each node's gate (`TopologyNode.Gate`) and the digest commits to it (written only for gated nodes, so an ungated flow keeps its digest). `audit.ApprovalEvidence`, `audit.VerifyApprovals` and `bide audit verify-approvals -call node:<name>` verify a gated node's decisions, with the node's record as the result; `bide view` shows the gate on the node (waiting, approved, signatures verified). The flows TLA+ model checks that a gated node never runs before k approvals, across crashes (`GateHeld`, `GatedResults`, `TallySound`, regressions `gate-missing`, `gate-unscoped` and `gate-request-order`). Runnable: `examples/approval/flow`. Journal format: one new record name, with no format version change before 1.0.
- `bide view -config flow.yaml` shows a flow definition without running it: the declared graph, approval gates ("2 of 3: ops, finance, risk") and every problem marked on its node, edge or arm; `-check` prints `file:node: message` lines and exits 1 on errors (`-strict`: on warnings too), for CI; `examples/plan/flows` holds a sample config and one with a marked problem. From the command line the check is structural; package `plan/planview` serves the same page (and `Check`) with a program's `plan.Registry`, for `plan.Load`'s full checks and the digest the flow journals. Both use the new `plan.InspectConfig`. The command reads YAML configs too.
- `bide demo`, a one-command crash demo (no API key): a payment agent with a 2-of-3 approval-gated charge runs in a child process over an audited SQLite journal; the demo kills the process after the charge reaches a fake provider and before its result is journaled, the restart halts with `OutcomeUnknown` instead of charging again, the demo resolves it from the provider's record (`ResolveHalt`), verifies the evidence, the anchored head and the approval signatures, and serves the run in the bide view page. CI runs it headless and fails unless the provider holds exactly one charge. The README opens with a recording of it (`assets/crash-demo.gif`, regenerated by `assets/demo/record.sh`).
- A plan flow run's first drive journals its declared topology under `flow:topology` (`plan.TopologyStep`), as `Topology` JSON, one Insert just before `flow:digest`; a resume writes neither again, and a run started earlier is not given one. `Topology` gains the digest's inputs (each node's `block`, each arm's `pred`, and `*_canonical` types with package paths), and `Flow.Digest` is computed through the new `plan.TopologyDigest`, so the journaled digest recomputes from the journaled topology; `plan.JournaledTopology` reads the record and checks it. The record is informational: `Run`, resume, routing and recovery do not read it, and `Conform` checks its digest. `bide view` draws a flow run from it with no flags ("Topology from journal (digest verified)"); `-topology` stays as an override and for older runs, and its digest is computed from the file when `-digest` is not given. Journal format: one new record name, with no format version change before 1.0.

### Changed

- **Breaking:** approver ids are printable ASCII (bytes 0x21 to 0x7E: letters, digits and punctuation, so emails and URIs are valid ids). `ApprovalPolicy.Validate` refuses any other id (a space, a control character, anything outside ASCII) with `ErrConfig` naming it, and two ids that differ only by ASCII case, naming both. This replaces the NFKC case-folding comparison: it also refuses homoglyphs (a Cyrillic "о" for a Latin "o"), which NFKC case folding let through, and it drops the core module's last dependency, `golang.org/x/text`, so the core module requires only the standard library. A plan flow config's `approval` block and `bide audit verify-approvals -approvers` follow the same rule.
- Homebrew: `brew install bide-ai/tap/bide` installs the `bide` command on macOS and Linux. The release workflow generates the formula from the release's `checksums.txt` (`scripts/brew-formula.sh`, self-tested in CI) and pushes it to bide-ai/homebrew-tap, replacing the hand-maintained `bide-audit` formula and recording the rename (`formula_renames.json`), so `brew upgrade` moves `bide-audit` users to `bide`.
- Release binaries are reproducible: `.goreleaser.yaml` builds with `-trimpath`, `-buildvcs=false` and commit-time file stamps, the release workflow pins Go 1.27.2 (`GOTOOLCHAIN=local`), and a new `repro` job rebuilds every published binary from the tag on macOS and compares the bytes (`scripts/repro.sh vX.Y.Z`, which anyone can run). `scripts/repro.sh --self-test` runs in CI: two builds of `cmd/bide` from copies of the tree at different paths are identical, and `.goreleaser.yaml` uses the flags the script rebuilds with. The archives also carry LICENSE, NOTICE and README.md. The security model documents the audit-only build (`go install -tags bide_audit_only ...`) and the rebuild.
- Docs follow the product's order (start, build, inspect, prove). The README is the pitch: the hero recording, the four guarantees, a four-step start (`bide demo`, `bide init`, `bide view`, `bide audit verify-evidence`) and a documentation map; its API sections (graphs through federated governance) moved, unchanged, to [A tour of bide](docs/tour.md), and each translation's to `docs/i18n/tour.<lang>.md`. The flows guide is now [Plan flows](docs/guides/plan-flows.md) (the old path redirects), and the docs index and the site's navigation are grouped as Start here, Concepts, Guides (build, inspect, prove and govern), Reference (with the CLI reference) and Proofs.
- **Breaking:** one command, `bide`, replaces `bide-audit`, `bide-view` and `bide-demo` ([design note](docs/design/tooling-and-onboarding.md)). `bide demo`, `bide view` (every `bide-view` flag), `bide check [-strict] <file>...` (the `bide-view -config -check` check, for several files), `bide audit <verb>` (every `bide-audit` verb and flag, with the same exit statuses), `bide version [-json]` and `bide help [command]`. Each command parses its flags with the standard library `flag` package, so command lines keep their single-dash flags; usage errors exit 2 and `bide help <command>` or `bide <command> -h` exit 0 (`bide audit <verb> -h` still exits 2, since audit reserves 0 for verified). Messages and the `-json` report name `bide audit` / `bide view`; `bide audit -version` and `bide version` print `bide <version>`. The module is `github.com/bide-ai/bide/cmd/bide` (`go install github.com/bide-ai/bide/cmd/bide@latest`; Homebrew `bide-ai/tap/bide`); its direct requirements are pinned by a test to bide's modules and the YAML library. Built with `-tags bide_audit_only` it holds only `audit`, `version` and `help` and links nothing outside the standard library and the core module, which a test checks. `docs/reference/cli.md` is generated from the commands' flag sets and a test fails when it is stale. `cmd/bide-view` is tagged once more at v0.14.0 as a deprecated module whose program only points to `bide view`, and is removed afterwards; `cmd/bide-audit` and the never-tagged `cmd/bide-demo` are removed. The release archives are `bide_<version>_<os>_<arch>`.
- The `bide-audit` command's code moved from `cmd/bide-audit` to `internal/auditcli` (`auditcli.Main`); `cmd/bide-audit` is a three-line program over it, so the command, its flags, output and exit statuses are unchanged. A new allowlist test, `TestDepsAreCore`, fails if the audit command's import graph reaches past the standard library and the core module. This prepares the single `bide` command ([design note](docs/design/tooling-and-onboarding.md)).
- The core module no longer requires `golang.org/x/sync`: the agent loop's parallel tool calls run on `internal/errgroup`, a standard-library equivalent of the errgroup subset it used (`WithContext`, `SetLimit`, `Go`, `Wait`, with the same semantics). `Group.SetLimit` panics when called while functions are running, as x/sync's does (without the panic, they would release their slots into the new semaphore and block). Nested modules that required `x/sync` only through the core no longer list it.

## [0.13.0] - 2026-10-12

### Changed

- `govern`, its log adapters, `integration` and `examples/govern` require gsm v0.14.0 (was v0.13.0), and the gsm machine gate pins its release commit. gsm v0.14.0 adds the XU check for deployments that merge component projections; bide replays one shared event log (gsm's `FedMachine` model), so it does not rely on XU. Nothing that built on v0.13.0 fails to build, and every governance example runs unchanged.
- The doccheck allowlist is empty (it held 44 entries): every exported identifier the check covers has a doc comment, and CI keeps the list empty ([#178]).
- Runnable `Example` tests for the main API: an agent with a tool, resuming a run, `ReadOnly` and side-effect tools after a lost result, an approval gate, `ResolveHalt`, and `audit.NewAuditedStore` ([#179]).
- Godoc describes current behavior only: P-numbers, "the 1.0 rewrite" and a migration note are removed, and the godoc of `WithSafety` no longer mentions a Safety argument of `Func` ([#178]).
- The README shows the chaos results as the benchmark card: bide never repeated the charge, while LangGraph (SQLite and Postgres), adk-go, trpc-agent-go, langchaingo and eino each charged again ([#175]).
- README section 4 and the governance guide frame gsm's theory as convergence by compensation, with the exact-regime-map line from normalization-confluence ([#176]).
- README section 4 is shortened to the guarantee and its scope; the details are in the governance guide and known limitations ([#183]).
- README: plainer wording; no claims changed. "The craft underneath" is now "Other details", and unverified comparisons with other projects are removed; the translations match.

### Added

- A run viewer guide (`docs/guides/run-viewer.md`, also on the docs site): installing and running `bide-view`, its flags, what each part of the page means, the crash, resolve and verify demo, and what it cannot show, with screenshots; a short section in the README and its translations. `bide-view -h` prints a synopsis and the exit statuses, and `sqlite.OpenReadOnly`'s godoc covers reading beside a writer.
- A run that pauses at an approval gate journals `approval-pending:<call>` (`agent.ApprovalPendingStep`, an `agent.ApprovalWait` with the call and the gate's k-of-n and approvers), once, in the drive whose model turn made the call. It is informational: no decision, resume or recovery reads it. `bide-view` shows such a run as "waiting for approval" and lists its gate before any decision. `approval-pending:` is a reserved step-name prefix, so a user `Step` named with it is `ErrConfig`.
- `bide-view` (`go install github.com/bide-ai/bide/cmd/bide-view@v0.13.0`, its own module): a read-only local page for one run, served on a loopback address only: an agent run's timeline (model turns, tool calls with their Safety, attempts, approvals with k-of-n status, halts with their `HaltRef`, end state), a plan flow's declared topology with the run's path and conformance, and whether the journal verifies against a signed tree head or evidence package; with `-approver-keys`, whether each m-of-n approval gate's signatures verify (`audit.VerifyApprovals`, as `bide-audit verify-approvals` runs it), shown in the Approval gates table and the AUDIT PROOF card with the reason for any failure, against the recorded policy or, with `-need` and `-approvers`, against the policy you expect, flagging a recorded policy that differs. It reads a SQLite file (`-db`), a Postgres journal (`-postgres`), or exported journals (`-journal`). `sqlite.OpenReadOnly` and `postgres.OpenReadOnly` open a journal without writing to it. `examples/approval` gains `run -crash` and `resolve`, and `examples/plan` gains `-topology-out`, for the demo in the debugging guide.
- A LangGraph crash-injection harness for the chaos benchmark, on SQLite and on Postgres (`PostgresSaver`) ([#166], [#167]).
- Design only, nothing implemented: a design note on probes for unknown outcomes and TLA+ model 13 (`spec/tla/probes`), which found that a provider's void must be monotonic: a void that sets rather than raises the watermark lets a stalled prober re-open an attempt a newer prober voided ([#181], [#182]).

## [0.12.0] - 2026-10-05

### Fixed

- Correction: the godoc of `govern.FederatedGovernor` and `govern.FederatedEventTool` said cross-registry conflicts resolve without coordination by the authority argument. That is false (normalization-confluence `FederationGRS.v`, `fed_thm_fed_convergence_refuted`). The authority argument makes the federated repair terminate in a unique valid normal form; event order across registries is safe because gsm v0.13.0's `Federation.Build` checks cross-registry CC (C1) and repaired CC (C2) and rejects a federation that fails either. Under gsm v0.12.0, which bide required, a federation could build whose agreed state depended on which event was appended first. The godoc, README, governance guide, concepts and known limitations now say so ([#172]).
- Documentation: the governance guide called `Federation.CoordinationPlan` minimal. It is a correct plan of size at most the number of independent cycles, not necessarily the minimum, and each point names the registry its cycles are driven from (`CoordinationPoint.Authority`) ([#172]).

- Documentation: the formal verification overview said the TLA+ models caught 29 bugs, all before release. They found 28 and confirmed one more found in review, and four (L1, S1, S2, S4) shipped in releases up to v0.9.0 and were fixed in v0.10.0. The overview, the README and the docs index now say so.

### Changed

- `govern`, its log adapters, `integration` and `examples/govern` require gsm v0.13.0 (was v0.12.0), which checks event order across federated registries (C1, C2) and reports delivery obligations; no federation in bide's examples fails the new checks. gsm behavior changes that reach bide's API: `Machine.Apply` normalizes an invalid input before applying the event, and `Embed` no longer copies `AllowMonotoneCycles` to the parent ([gsm v0.13.0 release notes](https://github.com/blackwell-systems/gsm/releases/tag/v0.13.0)). The gsm machine gate is pinned to the v0.13.0 release commit ([#172]).
- The README and governance guide state convergence under causal delivery and the exact scope of gsm's in-process oracle gate (a federation is oracle-gated per component only) ([#172], folding in [#170]).

### Added

- `govern.ConfluenceCertificate` records gsm's delivery obligations: `PairsUndeclared`, `CausalOrderRequired` (undeclared pairs that do not commute, so need causal order), `NotIdempotent` (events a duplicate delivery would change) and `Saturations`, with the new types `govern.EventPair` and `govern.Saturation`; `String()` reports them. `bide-audit verify-convergence` and `verify-run` print them without changing the verdict. A certificate written by an earlier version still verifies, with a note that it records no obligations; a certificate this version writes has keys an earlier `bide-audit` rejects, so verify it with this release's `bide-audit` or later ([#172]).

## [0.11.1] - 2026-10-04

### Fixed

- A failed model call's error said `(model) (model)`: the run wrapped `ErrModel` around an adapter error that already wrapped it. It wraps it only when it is missing ([#160]).
- `model/openai` with an empty API key: a server that needs a key refuses the request with 401 or 403, which read like a bad key or a provider fault. The `*agent.APIError` now says no API key was set (`openai.New` was given an empty key); a server that needs none, such as Ollama, works as before ([#160]).

### Changed

- Getting started works without an API key: an `agenttest` scripted model runs the first agent offline. The install step gets the `agent` package (`go get github.com/bide-ai/bide/agent@latest`), since getting the module root left `go.sum` incomplete; the core examples run with `go run github.com/bide-ai/bide/examples/<name>@latest`; and the guide says what a run prints and how a crashed run continues ([#161]).
- The examples name a `*agent.Journal` `journal`, not `store`, so `agent.New(model, journal, ...)` does not read like the removed `New(model, store, tools...)` ([#159]).

## [0.11.0] - 2026-10-04

### Changed

- **Breaking:** the API redesign's new names (P15, docs/design/api-v1.md section 10.2, [#156]). The transitional names and the old API they stood beside are gone. The list below is the upgrade path, name by name; a module that used `github.com/bide-ai/bide/mcp` requires `github.com/bide-ai/bide/mcptools` instead.

  - Construction: `Build` is `New(model, journal, opts...) (*Agent, error)`; the old `New(model, store, tools...)` and the builder methods (`Use`, `UseTool`, `WithMaxTurns`, `SetMaxConcurrency`, ...) are removed: pass `WithTools`, `WithMiddleware`, `WithToolMiddleware` and the other options, or derive a copy with `With`. `New` makes every check `Build` made (two tools with one name, a non-object schema, an m-of-n policy's keys), so a configuration problem fails at construction rather than at a run.
  - Runs: `RunMessage`, `StreamMessage`, `ResumeRun`, `RunTypedMessage` and `Session.SendMessage`/`SendMessageOnce` are `Run`, `Stream`, `Resume`, `RunTyped` and `Session.Send`/`SendOnce`, each taking a `Message` (`agent.UserText(s)`) and run options and returning a `*Result`. The string entry points they replaced (`Run` and `Stream` on a string, `RunSaga`, `RunResult`, `RunSagaResult`, `StreamSaga`, the `RunTyped` and `RunTypedNative` functions, `AgentStream.Final`) are removed: a saga is `Run(..., WithSaga())`, native structured output is `WithOutputMode(OutputNative)`. `AgentStream` and `AgentEvent` are `RunStream` and `RunEvent`; `audit.Record` is `audit.RecordStream`, which returns `(*agent.Result, error)`.
  - Journal: the `Durable` interface and the stores' `Do`, `History` and `Journal` shims are removed. Every function that took a `Durable` takes a `*Journal` (`agent.NewJournal(store)`); a test fake that wrapped a `Durable` wraps the `Store` instead (and implements `Unwrap() Store`, which `Capability` and the run's identity follow). The journal verbs are generic methods: `j.Step`, `j.Parallel`, `j.Signal`, `j.Enqueue` (the channel `Send`) and `j.AnswerInterrupt` (the `Resume[T]` wrapper).
  - Pauses and halts: the aliases `PendingApproval`, `Interrupted`, `Awaiting`, `Sleeping` and `ResumeHalt` are `ApprovalPending`, `InterruptPending`, `SignalPending`, `TimerPending` and `OutcomeUnknown`; `ResolveHaltRef` is `ResolveHalt(ctx, j, HaltRef, Outcome)`, and the wrappers `ResolveStepHalt` and `ApproveAs` are removed (`SubmitDecision` records one approver's decision).
  - Context decorators: `ContextWithIdentity`, `ContextWithWaker` and `ContextWithClock` are removed; pass `WithIdentity`, `WithWaker` or `WithClock` as a run option. `WithIdentity` refuses an empty identity (`ErrConfig`), which `ContextWithIdentity` bound.
  - Tools: the `Tool` interface is `Spec() ToolSpec` and `Call`; the old method set (`Name`, `Description`, `ArgsSchema`, `Safety`) and `SpecOf` are removed. `Func` takes its safety as an option (`WithSafety`) and, like `CompensatedFunc`, `SubAgent` and `RetrievalTool`, returns `(Tool, error)`; `MustFunc`, `MustCompensatedFunc`, `MustSubAgent` and `MustRetrievalTool` panic instead. `SubAgent(nil)` is `ErrConfig`. A decorator that embeds a `Tool` inherits its `Spec`, and with it the gate and the timeout; it overrides in its own `Spec` (`s := d.Tool.Spec()`, then the fields it changes). `New`, and a plan flow registering a tool, refuse (`ErrConfig`) a tool whose `Name`, `Description`, `ArgsSchema` (compared as JSON) or `Safety` method disagrees with its `Spec`, including a pointer-receiver method on a tool registered by value, one of another signature, and one two embedded fields declare; a method of one of those names that means something else is refused too (rename it): written for the old interface, such a method no longer overrides anything (a decorator's `Safety()` declaring a side effect would be ignored, and a resume would run it again).
  - Packages: `mcp` is `mcptools` (`github.com/bide-ai/bide/mcptools`); the scripted model and its turns (`ScriptedModel`, `NewScriptedModel`, `ToolTurn`, `TextTurn`, `ErrorTurn`) moved to `agent/agenttest`, beside `MemJournal`, `MustJournal`, `MustNew`, `Must`, `Answer` and `IdentityContext`; plan's `Register*` functions are methods of `*plan.Registry`.
- **Breaking:** `Run` without `WithSaga` on a run journaled as a saga drives it as the saga it is (its rollback included), where `RunSaga`'s absence was `ErrConfig`; `WithSaga` on a run that is not a saga is still `ErrConfig`. A session turn and a sub-agent still refuse a drive that differs.
- **Breaking:** `audit.AuditedStore` wraps a `Store` (not a `Durable`): it anchors after every `Insert` but the journal header's (also one that found its entry stored, or failed: an A3 write that committed while reporting an error is anchored), the header together with the run's first record, and never a head of the header alone; a publish that failed is covered by the run's next write (a replay no longer retries it), and `AuditedStore.Reanchor(ctx, runID)` anchors a run whose last write's publish failed (call it on `OnError`). `cmd/bide-audit` refuses an export whose first record is not a journal header.

### Fixed

- An `audit.AttenuatingSubAgent` delegation whose sub-run held only its journal header (its first record failed to write) was taken for a sub-run with records but no journaled authority, and every later call refused it; a sub-run holding only its header is now one that has not started.

### Added

- `audit.AuditedStore.Reanchor(ctx, runID)`: anchors a run's journal now if no published head covers it, the recovery for a run whose last write's publish failed, or whose write landed under a context that was done, which `Insert` cannot anchor and reports to `OnError` with the context's error (`OnError` is the signal).
- CI checks that no removed name appears in Go code, godoc or docs (`internal/tools/oldnames`).

## [0.10.0] - 2026-10-03

### Added

#### Run API, cancellation and recovery dispatch (P14)

- The Run API, under transitional names (P14): `Agent.RunMessage(ctx, runID, input Message, opts...)`, `Agent.ResumeRun(ctx, runID, opts...)`, `Agent.StreamMessage` with `AgentStream.Result()`, `Agent.RunTypedMessage[T]` (a Go 1.27 generic method), `Session.SendMessage` and `Session.SendMessageOnce`, which the 1.0 rewrite renames `Run`, `Resume`, `Stream`, `RunTyped`, `Send` and `SendOnce`. Each returns a `Result` whenever the run ID is valid (`agent.ValidateRunID`), whatever the error; `Result.Output` holds a typed run's answer as journaled. The input is a `Message`, so a run can start from text and images ([#138]).
- Run options (P14): the agent-and-run options (`WithMaxTurns`, `WithTokenBudget`, `WithSystemPrompt`, `WithSampling`, `WithToolChoice`, `WithWaker`, `WithIdentity`, `WithMaxConcurrency`, `WithClock`) now apply per run, and `WithSaga()`, `WithToolFilter(names...)` and `WithOutputMode(OutputTool | OutputNative)` are new. The journaling rule: a run's first drive journals its input and options in `run:start` (`RunStart` gains `Session`, `Typed`, `Settings`, `Principal`, `Tools` and `Ext`, and the kind `session_turn`), and every later drive, a recovery drive included, runs under them. A later drive's different turn limit or token budget is journaled as an amendment `run:limits:<n>`; any other different setting (input, saga, tool filter, system prompt, sampling, tool choice, output mode, typed schema, principal) is `ErrConfig`, before any model call. The tool filter is enforced at dispatch: a call outside it records an error result and never runs (model 10, finding L5). The principal (`OnBehalfOf`, `AuthorityRef`) is journaled and restored; the `Actor` stays live ([#138]).
- `agent.Cancel(ctx, j, runID, reason)` and `agent.Status(ctx, j, runID)` (`RunStatus`, `RunState`) (P14, D1 and D8). `Cancel` writes `run:cancelled`, or on a saga the rollback request `run:cancel-requested`, which the saga's drive answers by rolling the run back and writing `run:cancelled`. A drive checks for the cancellation when it starts (and again once it has written `run:start` or a limit amendment, or lost the `run:start` insert to another drive: model 10's `DStart` and `DAmend` return to `DOpen`), at every turn boundary after its first, and after each won side-effect claim, before the call (model 10, L2); calls in flight finish, and a retry-safe call already dispatched in the current turn may still run (no `Get` per retry-safe call). A sub-run (a sub-agent's, a `SubRunFor` run, a delegation) reads its tree root's cancellation too, at each of those checks and from the root's own store (a sub-agent outside a saga may journal to another), so a `Cancel` of the root stops the whole tree (model 11, `NoFireAfterRootCancel`). A `plan` flow honours `Cancel`: its open reads its end markers, a node reads `run:cancelled` once it will run (after a side-effect node's claim is won), and its `run:complete` is read back. The first end marker in journal order is a run's end for every reader, and each writer of one reads the markers back (L3). `Cancel` of a run already cancelled returns nil, of a run otherwise over `ErrRunEnded`. `Status` reads one `Load` (L6). New errors: `ErrRunCancelled`, `ErrRunEnded`, `ErrNotStarted` and `ErrNotResumable`, none in a category (`ErrNotStarted` is often a race with a run's first drive) ([#138]).
- Recovery dispatch (P14): `agent.Resumer` (`func(ctx, runID, start RunStart) error`), `agent.ResumeAgent(a, opts...)`, `agent.ResumeTyped[T](a, opts...)` and `agent.ResumeAny(rs...)`. A recovery pass reads each run's `run:start` under its lease; a run with none is skipped and reported once per process (`ErrNotStarted`), and read again on every pass (L7); a run no `Resumer` drives is reported once (`ErrNotResumable`); the process remembers its 65,536 most recent reports. A run a pass drove to its cancelled end (a saga's cancellation rollback included) is recovered, not a failure. `ResumeAgent` takes only the deployment's options (a Waker, a clock, a concurrency cap, an identity's `Actor`) ([#138]).

#### Sessions

- Sessions (P14, model 12's S3): a Send turn whose run was cancelled is recorded closed, with no answer and outside the transcript, by the next message's `Send`; `Send` of the cancelled message (the same text) returns `ErrRunCancelled`. A saga turn's `Cancel` writes only its rollback request: the next message's `Send` drives the turn's rollback itself, under the turn lease and without holding the session handle's mutex (other callers on the handle are not blocked behind its compensators: while it is in progress, another worker or a second caller on the same handle gets `ErrTurnContended`), and then reads the journal again and records the turn closed. Over a store with no `Leaser`, the handle's mutex stays held across the rollback, so two callers on one handle never drive it at once ([#138]).
- `agent.ErrTurnContended`: a `Session.Send` or `SendOnce` whose turn run another holder leases returns at once with an error wrapping it, having driven nothing (no category, like `ErrLeaseLost`); send the message again later. `Agent.Session(ctx, id, opts...)` takes `LeaseOption` values (`WithLeaseHolder`, `WithLeaseTTL`) for that lease ([#137]).

#### Tools (P12)

- `agent.ToolSpec` (`Name`, `Title`, `Description`, `Input`, `Output`, `Safety`, `Approval`, `Timeout`), everything the agent knows about a tool, and `agent.SpecOf(t)`, which reads a tool's `Spec() ToolSpec` method or, for a tool without one, its `Name`, `Description`, `ArgsSchema` and `Safety` (deprecated from the start: see Deprecated). The agent reads each tool's spec once, when it is registered, and decides every call from that copy ([#117]).
- Tool options: `agent.ToolOption` with `WithSafety`, `WithApproval`, `WithTimeout`, `WithTitle` and `WithOutputSchema`, and `agent.SingleApproval()`, the one-decision gate. `Func` and `CompensatedFunc` take trailing options; `SubAgent(name, description, sub, opts...)` takes `WithApproval`, so a parent can require approval before it delegates, and refuses `WithSafety` and `WithTimeout`. An invalid option panics with an error wrapping `ErrConfig`, as `Func` does for an argument type it cannot describe, until the 1.0 rewrite returns errors ([#117]).
- Tool timeouts (`WithTimeout`, `ToolSpec.Timeout`): the call, tool middleware included, runs under the deadline. A result returned after the deadline is recorded; an error returned after it has an unknown outcome: a side effect records nothing, fails the drive with `ErrToolOutcomeUnknown` and halts on resume, and a retry-safe tool records the error. It bounds only tools that honor their context ([#117]).
- `agent.ToolCall` (`Use`, `Spec`, `RunID`) and `ToolCall.ErrorText(err)`, what tool middleware receives ([#117]).
- `agent.ErrToolNotCalled`, a condition (no category) for a tool call known never to have reached its tool. A tool middleware that ends a call without calling `next` returns an error wrapping it, and only then; `middleware.ToolRateLimit` and `middleware.ToolRetry` do. The agent tracks each call's state by compare-and-swap (reached, refused by the base handler, or closed when the chain returned without entering it): a call counts as not called only when refused, or closed with `ErrToolNotCalled`; any other closed call leaves a side effect's outcome unknown and the run halts. The base handler refuses an invocation that comes after the chain returned. The `ToolMiddleware` contract: reach the tool only through `next` ([#117]).
- Tool-result, denial and saga-failure records journal the `Safety` and approval gate the call ran under (`Record.Safety`, `Record.Approval`); a saga rollback reads the recorded safety ([#117]).
- A tool that wraps another says so with an `Unwrap() Tool` method; the agent follows it to find a wrapped `SubAgent`, so a saga rollback and the tree's token budget recurse into its sub-run ([#117]).
- `mcp.WithApproval(name, policy)`: `Tools` fails with `ErrConfig` when the policy is nil or invalid, or when the server lists no tool of that name, so a misspelt gate never leaves the real tool ungated. Each MCP tool's spec: `Title` (its title, else its annotations' title), `Output` (its `outputSchema`) and `Timeout` (`WithCallTimeout`) ([#117]).
- `govern.EventToolConfig` and `govern.FederatedEventToolConfig`, whose `Options` pass `agent.ToolOption`s to the tool; `audit.AttenuationConfig`, and trailing `agent.ToolOption`s on `audit.AttenuatingSubAgent`, which go to its `SubAgent` ([#117]).

#### Construction under Build (P13)

- `agent.Build(model, journal, opts...) (*Agent, error)`, construction from options, and `Agent.With(opts...)`, which returns a configured copy and leaves the agent unchanged. `Build` and `With` return every configuration problem as `ErrConfig` when the agent is built: a nil model, journal, option, tool, middleware or function; two tools with one name or one named `final_answer`; a tool name the agent's model refuses, when the model declares its rule (`ToolRules`); a non-object input schema; an invalid approval policy, an m-of-n policy with no `WithApproverVerifiers` or with two approvers on one signing key; a negative limit; a `WithRetrieval` k below 1; and a tool choice with an unknown mode or a tool the agent lacks. `Build` is the transitional name of the 1.0 `New`. `Agent.Journal()` returns the agent's journal ([#127]).
- Agent options: `WithTools`, `WithMiddleware`, `WithToolMiddleware`, `WithApproverVerifiers`, `WithToolErrorRedactor`, `WithSystemPromptFunc` (its function gets the run's `RunInfo` and may fail), `WithRetrieval`, `WithOptions`, and `WithMaxTurns`, `WithTokenBudget`, `WithSystemPrompt`, `WithSampling`, `WithToolChoice`, `WithWaker`, `WithIdentity`, `WithMaxConcurrency` and `WithClock`. The last value given for a setting wins; `WithSystemPrompt` and `WithSystemPromptFunc` share one slot. The agent's `WithIdentity`, `WithWaker` and `WithClock` apply to a run whose context carries none ([#127]).
- Option scopes are interfaces with unexported methods (`Option`, `RunOption`, `ParallelOption`, `StepOption`, `ResolveOption`, `LeaseOption`, `RecoverOption`, `RecoverLoopOption`, `RetrievalOption`, and `ToolOption`), and a setting for several scopes returns one of the combination types `AgentRunOption`, `ConcurrencyOption`, `ClockOption`, `SafetyOption` or `LeaseControl`, so an option passed where it does not apply does not compile. `RunOption` values are type-checked now and taken by the run API in the next redesign step ([#127]).
